  - **The client configuration endpoint's refusals and their challenges.** The endpoint is an OAuth 2.0 protected resource ([RFC 7592 Section 2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7592#section-2)), and the 401 answers of its read, update and delete operations carry one `Bearer` challenge, attached by `CredentialEndpointDpopValidation.Challenged`, the body the Global Token Revocation endpoint's refusals are challenged through, reached through `CredentialEndpointDpopValidation.ChallengedAsync`, which resolves the issuer once for the refusal; its `realm` is the issuer the request is served under. [RFC 9110 Section 15.5.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110#section-15.5.2): "The server generating a 401 response MUST send a WWW-Authenticate header field (Section 11.6.1) containing at least one challenge applicable to the target resource."
    - A request whose `Authorization` field holds no Bearer token, as `BearerTokenValidation.TryExtractBearer` reads it (no field, an empty or white-space value, a credential of another scheme), lacks the authentication information the endpoint reads and is answered 401 with an empty body and the challenge `Bearer realm="<issuer>"`, which carries no error code. [RFC 7592 Section 2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7592#section-2): "The client MUST use its registration access token in all calls to this endpoint as an OAuth 2.0 Bearer Token [RFC6750]." [RFC 6750 Section 3.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6750#section-3.1): "If the request lacks any authentication information (e.g., the client was unaware that authentication is necessary or attempted using an unsupported authentication method), the resource server SHOULD NOT include an error code or other error information."
    - A registration access token the application's `ValidateRegistrationAccessTokenAsync` refuses is answered 401, the body's error `invalid_token` with the description "Registration access token is invalid.", and the challenge `Bearer realm="<issuer>", error="invalid_token"`, at read, update and delete alike. A deletion whose client another party deletes or replaces while it runs is answered as a lost race (400 `invalid_client_metadata`, "The registration changed during this deletion."), and a deletion sent after it is answered by the state it finds. [RFC 7592 Section 2.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7592#section-2.1), and in the same words [Section 2.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7592#section-2.2) and [Section 2.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7592#section-2.3): "If the registration access token used to make this request is not valid, the server MUST respond with an error as described in the OAuth Bearer Token Usage specification [RFC6750]." [RFC 6750 Section 3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6750#section-3): "If the protected resource request included an access token and failed authentication, the resource server SHOULD include the "error" attribute to provide the client with the reason why the access request was declined."
    - The 400 for an `Authorization` field sent on two lines carries the challenge `Bearer realm="<issuer>", error="invalid_request"`; the other 400 and 500 answers carry no challenge, and neither does an accepted request's answer.
    - A registration that is not a `ClientRecord` when the endpoint runs (the dispatcher sets the registration before a candidate runs, and an application's materializer may replace it with a record of another family) is answered 500 `server_error` with `WellKnownOAuthErrorDescriptions.ServerNotReady` and no challenge, before the token is read, since the fault is the server's wiring and not the caller's. [RFC 9110 Section 15.6.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110#section-15.6.1): "The 500 (Internal Server Error) status code indicates that the server encountered an unexpected condition that prevented it from fulfilling the request."
    - The challenged answers need the issuer resolvable, as the credential endpoint's refusals do: a client the endpoint registers carries no issuer of its own, so the application's issuer resolver or the request's context names it; where neither does, the default resolver throws, and the host's HTTP application answers the refusal 500 `server_error` without a challenge.
  - **The management operations act on the registration as stored.** The read and the update are built from the registration as stored, loaded again through `LoadRegistrationAsync`, not from the record an application's materialization answered for the request, so nothing a materialization changes is answered as registered or written to the store. The update sets the RFC 7591 metadata members it sets and no other member of the stored record, its conditional replacement is against the stored revision, and the update notification receives the stored record as the earlier one. The read, the update and the deletion each make one pass, in one order: the `Authorization` field is read, and a request without a Bearer token is challenged with no store call; the registration as stored is loaded; the registration access token is checked through `ValidateRegistrationAccessTokenAsync` for that stored record's tenant and client identifier; and the operation acts on that record: the read answers the metadata of the revision it loaded, the update replaces it conditioned on that revision, and the deletion deletes it conditioned on that revision. A change another party commits between the load and the conditional write fails the write: the update's conditional replacement, answered 400 `invalid_client_metadata` with the description "The registration changed during this update.", and the deletion's conditional deletion, answered 400 `invalid_client_metadata` with the description "The registration changed during this deletion." and no event; neither loads the registration or checks the token again, and a deletion the client sends again is authenticated against the record it finds. A replacement or revocation of a registration access token is such a change only when the application commits it as the registration's next revision, which is the application's to guarantee (`IClientRegistrationStore`). What a materialization can change of these steps is the registration they start from: the tenant the load is asked for and the client identifier the stored record must name are those of the context's registration, which the materialization answered. The field, the stored record, the token check and the record the operation acts on are the request's, the store's and the application's seam's, so a materialization that names another client of the same tenant is answered as a stored record naming another client, one that names another tenant has that tenant's record loaded and the presented token checked for it, and no materialization makes an operation act on a record other than the one whose tenant and client identifier the token was checked for. A stored record that is gone, or names another client, is answered 401 `invalid_token` with the challenge, as a refused token is. [RFC 7592 Section 2.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7592#section-2.1): "If the client does not exist on this server, the server MUST respond with HTTP 401 Unauthorized and the registration access token used to make this request SHOULD be immediately revoked." [RFC 7592 Section 2.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7592#section-2.2): "If the client does not exist on this server, the server MUST respond with HTTP 401 Unauthorized, and the registration access token used to make this request SHOULD be immediately revoked." [RFC 7592 Section 2.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7592#section-2.2): "Valid values of client metadata fields in this request MUST replace, not augment, the values previously associated with this client." [RFC 7592 Section 3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7592#section-3): "Additionally, the authorization server MUST return all registered metadata about this client, including any fields provisioned by the authorization server itself."
    - Proven over the listener by `DynamicRegistrationEndToEndTests.AnUpdateUnderAMaterializationReplacesTheMetadataOfTheRegistrationAsStoredAsync` (a materialization that adds the token exchange capability, on a server wired to serve the token exchange grant, and one that gives `logo_uri`, a member the update does not set, a value: the PUT answered as without a materialization, the stored record keeping its capabilities and its `logo_uri`, and a later token exchange request with no materialization wired answered byte for byte as at a registration that never held the capability, `404`, where an update that wrote the added capability has that request served by the grant), `AnUpdateUnderAMaterializationAnnouncesTheRegistrationAsStoredAsTheEarlierOneAsync` (the one update notification's earlier registration the stored record, not the materialized one), `AReadUnderAMaterializationAnswersTheRegistrationAsStoredAsync` (the read answered byte for byte as without a materialization), `AnUpdateWhoseTokenIsReplacedAfterItsCheckWritesNothingAsync` (the token replaced and a revision committed after the seam answers valid: 400 "The registration changed during this update.", the other party's revision kept), `AReadWhoseTokenIsReplacedAfterItsCheckAnswersTheRevisionItCheckedAsync` (the read answering the revision it loaded), `ADeletionWhoseTokenIsReplacedBeforeItsDeletionCommitsLosesItsRaceAsync` (the token replaced and a revision committed after the check and before the deletion commits: 400 "The registration changed during this deletion.", the seam asked once, the other party's revision kept, and the deletion sent again with the token valid for the stored record answered 204), `ARequestWhoseTokenIsReplacedBeforeItsCheckIsAnInvalidTokenAsync` (read, update and delete, each answered as a refused token), `AManagementRequestWhoseClientIsNotStoredWhenItIsLoadedIsAnInvalidTokenAsync` (the read and the update of a client gone or of a stored record naming another client, each answered byte for byte as a refused token) and `ADeletionWhoseClientIsGoneWhileItRunsLosesItsRaceAsync` (the client removed after the check and before the deletion commits: 400 "The registration changed during this deletion.", the seam asked once, and the deletion sent again answered 401 `invalid_token` as a client that does not exist); and by `LiveServerAlterationTests.ConditionalDeletionKeepsCacheDeletedAcrossCommitAndDeliveryOrdersOverWire` (an update committed first: the deletion answered 400 "The registration changed during this deletion." with nothing committed and no event, the deletion sent again answered 204, and the tombstone one revision beyond the removed record retained in either delivery order).
  - **Deviations.** Each is named with its reason.
    - A management request whose client does not exist when it arrives meets no registration and is answered by `AuthorizationServerIntegration.ResolveMissingRegistrationAsync`, 401 `invalid_token` with no `WWW-Authenticate` field, where [RFC 9110 Section 15.5.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110#section-15.5.2) says: "The server generating a 401 response MUST send a WWW-Authenticate header field (Section 11.6.1) containing at least one challenge applicable to the target resource." The reason: no registration names the issuer there, and without an application issuer resolver or an issuer on the request's context none resolves for the realm.
      - Its consequences: a known client's refused token is challenged and an absent client's is not, so the answer tells a caller whether a `client_id` exists on the server; and the absent client's answer carries `invalid_token` also when the request carries no credential, where [RFC 6750 Section 3.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6750#section-3.1) says: "If the request lacks any authentication information (e.g., the client was unaware that authentication is necessary or attempted using an unsupported authentication method), the resource server SHOULD NOT include an error code or other error information."
  - Proven over the listener by `DynamicRegistrationEndToEndTests.AClientConfigurationRequestWithoutATokenIsChallenged` (no `Authorization` field: 401, an empty body, the literal challenge without `error`), `AClientConfigurationRequestWhoseAuthorizationFieldCarriesNoBearerTokenIsChallenged` (an empty value, a value of white space and a `Basic` credential: the same answer), `AClientConfigurationRequestPresentingItsTokenUnderTheDPoPSchemeIsChallengedWithoutAnErrorCode` (the client's valid registration access token under the `DPoP` scheme: the same answer, no client metadata), `AClientConfigurationRequestWhoseTokenIsRefusedIsAnInvalidToken` (401, `invalid_token`, the literal challenge with `error`), `ADeletionWhoseClientIsGoneWhileItRunsLosesItsRaceAsync` (the client removed between the request's authentication and its deletion: the lost race's 400 without a challenge, and the deletion sent again 401 `invalid_token` with no `WWW-Authenticate` field), `AClientConfigurationRequestServedUnderARegistrationTheEndpointCannotUseIsAServerError` (a materializer that answers a registration of another family, with and without a token: 500 `server_error`, no challenge, no fault reaching the host), `AClientConfigurationRefusalServedWhereNoIssuerResolvesIsAServerFault` (a refused token where no issuer resolves: the default resolver's throw, answered 500 `server_error` by the host, no challenge) and `AClientConfigurationReadWithItsTokenCarriesNoChallenge` (200, no `WWW-Authenticate` field); and by `LiveServerAlterationTests.MissingClientOrInvalidManagementBearerReturnsUnauthorizedOverWire` (a refused token of a known client at read, update and delete: 401, `invalid_token`, the literal challenge with `error`; an absent client's token: 401, `invalid_token`, no `WWW-Authenticate` field) and `DelayedDeletionPreservesReusedTenantOverWire` (the stale deletion: the lost race's 400 without a challenge and no event, the reused tenant's registration kept), each served with an application issuer resolver.

Decisions in this section are foundational enough that they need no re-discussion. Move items here from §7 when they're settled across the codebase and tests, or split into ADRs in `/documents/ADRs/` when they deserve their own decision record.

- **Live configuration.** Serving wiring changes through requested, ordered alterations. Admission captures one validated composition; the server drains requests, validates candidate copies, and publishes them atomically. Registration and policy data retain their application-owned consistency boundaries. See [Live configuration](#41-live-configuration).

- **The authorize evaluation sees the parsed authorization details.** `AuthorizationRequestEvaluation.RequestedAuthorizationDetailObjects` carries the same request's `RequestedAuthorizationDetails` string, parsed through the wired `ParseAuthorizationDetailListDelegate` into the neutral `AuthorizationDetail` model — the same parse the token endpoint's `ResolveCredentialAuthorizationDelegate` seam consumes — so an application correlating `issuer_state` to the requested `credential_configuration_id` reads the parsed value instead of re-parsing the string itself; a request carrying no `authorization_details` observes `null` for both. Proven by `RequestedAuthorizationDetailsReachTheDecisionSeamParsed` and `RequestWithoutAuthorizationDetailsObservesNullParsedList`.

- **Paired seams are refused at `Validate()`.** `AuthorizationServerIntegration.ValidateFeatureDependencies` refuses `ResolveCredentialAuthorizationAsync` wired without `ParseAuthorizationDetailsAsync`, and refuses the parser wired for the built-in `openid_credential` type alone without the resolver — the two shapes [RFC 9396 §5](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9396) can never validly reach, since a half-wired pair would otherwise answer `invalid_authorization_details` at the wire with no composition-time signal until a Wallet's first request. Coupled seams wire in one alteration (`AlterationCandidate` — see `RequestAlterationAsync`'s own documentation). Proven by `ResolveCredentialAuthorizationWiredWithoutParserIsRefused`, `ParserAloneForTheBuiltInTypeOnlyIsRefused`, and `BothAuthorizationDetailsSeamsWiredValidates`.

- **Unified key-rotation primitive.** Both signing and HMAC use the same slot model (`Incoming` / `Current` / `Retiring` / `Historical`) — signing via the existing `SigningKeySet` per `ClientRecord.SigningKeys[usage]`, HMAC via the non-generic `KeySet` (with `InProcessKeySet` as the in-process default). Selection (`SelectSigningKeyDelegate` / `SelectHmacKeyDelegate`) is separated from byte-loading (`ServerSigningKeyResolverDelegate` / `ServerVerificationKeyResolverDelegate` / `ResolveServerHmacKeyDelegate`). All byte-loaders take typed `(KeyId, TenantId, ExchangeContext, CancellationToken)`, the public-key loader with the expected `KeyUsageContext` after the `KeyId`. See §3 for the full layered model.
- **Token revocation (RFC 7009), server side.** The revocation endpoint is stateless (the same `StartsNewFlow` + `FlowKind.Stateless` shape as `client_credentials`, not the stateful correlation-key path). The library owns the wire — client authentication via `ValidateClientCredentialsDelegate`, the §2.2 empty-200 that never reveals token validity, and a fail-closed candidate gate (the endpoint materializes only when both `RevokeTokenDelegate` and `ValidateClientCredentialsDelegate` are wired). The application owns the token store behind `RevokeTokenDelegate`: it scopes revocation to the authenticated client's tokens and cascades refresh → access (§2.1). That store is the same state the per-call decision seams (`EvaluateAccessDelegate`, `LoadClientRegistrationDelegate`) read, so a client-driven RFC 7009 revocation, an admin action, and an inbound SSF/CAEP `session-revoked` signal all converge on one piece of state — continuous access evaluation is then an emergent property of the existing per-call seams, not a separate mechanism. First slice of the global-logout arc. Convergence requires the application to commit these triggers to the same coherent state read by request resolvers.
- **Global Token Revocation (draft-parecki-oauth-global-token-revocation), server side.** A stateless JSON-body command endpoint (`global_token_revocation_endpoint`) that revokes all of a subject's tokens by RFC 9493 Subject Identifier. The `sub_id` reuses `Verifiable.Core.SecurityEvents.SubjectIdentifier` (the same type the Shared Signals subsystem uses); the body crosses the serialization firewall through `ParseGlobalTokenRevocationRequestDelegate` (default JSON ships in `Verifiable.Json`, see below); the command drops out to `RevokeSubjectTokensDelegate`, whose returned outcome maps to the §3 status codes (204/403/404/422), with 400 (malformed/invalid `sub_id`) and 401 (auth) handled by the endpoint. Fail-closed gate: capability + parse seam + revoke seam + client auth all required. **There is deliberately no library "orchestrator" object** — the revoke-subject seam *is* the global-logout fan-out: the application revokes the subject's grants against its store and, when it runs a Shared Signals Transmitter, MAY emit a CAEP `session-revoked` event. This is the "command" end of the signal/command spectrum (CAEP being the best-effort "signal" end). Shipped as its own `GlobalTokenRevocationEndpoints.Builder` in `Verifiable.OAuth.Logout`, demonstrating the §2.2 new-endpoint extension shape. The default JSON `sub_id` parser ships in `Verifiable.Json` (`GlobalTokenRevocationJsonParsing` + `UseDefaultGlobalTokenRevocationJsonParsing`, reusing `SsfJsonReadHelpers.ReadSubject`); the endpoint is folded into the all-capabilities discovery-coherence test, and the happy-path flow test exercises the production parser end-to-end.
  - **The refusals and their challenges.** Two answers carry one `Bearer` challenge: a refusal of the client authentication seam (401) and the revoke-subject seam's `GlobalTokenRevocationOutcome.Forbidden` (403). The challenge is composed by `BearerTokenChallenge.BuildChallenge`, whose `realm` is the issuer the request is served under (the application's `ResolveIssuerAsync` when wired, the default resolver otherwise) written in its URI form as [RFC 3987 Section 3.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc3987#section-3.1) maps an IRI, the host in its IDNA ASCII form and every other character outside ASCII percent-encoded, so the quoted-string of [RFC 9110 Section 5.6.4](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110#section-5.6.4) carries it. [RFC 9110 Section 15.5.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110#section-15.5.2): "The server generating a 401 response MUST send a WWW-Authenticate header field (Section 11.6.1) containing at least one challenge applicable to the target resource." [RFC 6750 Section 3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6750#section-3): "If the protected resource request does not include authentication credentials or does not contain an access token that enables access to the protected resource, the resource server MUST include the HTTP "WWW-Authenticate" response header field; it MAY include it in response to other conditions as well." and "All challenges defined by this specification MUST use the auth-scheme value "Bearer"." The 400, 404 and 422 answers to a caller the seam authenticated carry no challenge, and neither does the 500 server fault: they are the other conditions of that first sentence, for which the endpoint does not take its MAY. The challenged answers need the issuer resolvable, as the credential endpoint's refusals do: with no application resolver, no issuer on the registration and none on the request's context the default resolver throws, and an issuer whose host IDNA cannot map makes the realm's derivation throw; the issuer is the application's own configuration, the one its tokens are issued under.
    - A request whose `Authorization` field holds an access token, as `BearerTokenValidation.TryExtractAccessToken` reads it (a token after the `Bearer` or the `DPoP` scheme), and which the seam refuses is answered 401, the body's error `invalid_token` with the description `WellKnownOAuthErrorDescriptions.ClientAuthenticationFailed`, and the challenge `Bearer realm="<issuer>", error="invalid_token"`. [RFC 6750 Section 3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6750#section-3): "If the protected resource request included an access token and failed authentication, the resource server SHOULD include the "error" attribute to provide the client with the reason why the access request was declined." [RFC 6750 Section 3.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6750#section-3.1), `invalid_token`: "The access token provided is expired, revoked, malformed, or invalid for other reasons. The resource SHOULD respond with the HTTP 401 (Unauthorized) status code."
    - A request whose client credentials are malformed (a client authentication field sent twice, the `Authorization` field on two field lines or outside its grammar, two mechanisms) is answered 400 `invalid_request` with the challenge `Bearer realm="<issuer>", error="invalid_request"` and never reaches the seam, which receives the one `ClientCredentialReading` the library read. A `Basic` credential the seam refuses is answered in its own scheme, as the token endpoint's client authentication failure is: 401 `invalid_client` with `Basic realm="<issuer>"` when an issuer resolves for the request, and otherwise 400 `invalid_client` without a challenge.
    - Every other request the seam refuses (no `Authorization` field, an empty or white-space value, a credential of another scheme than `Basic`) lacks the authentication information the endpoint reads and is answered 401 with an empty body and the challenge `Bearer realm="<issuer>"`, which carries no error code. [RFC 6750 Section 3.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6750#section-3.1): "If the request lacks any authentication information (e.g., the client was unaware that authentication is necessary or attempted using an unsupported authentication method), the resource server SHOULD NOT include an error code or other error information." A form credential (a client assertion or secret in the request's parameters) is handed to the seam, which may accept it; one the seam refuses is answered as a request without an `Authorization` field is, since the draft defines no form credential for this endpoint ([draft-parecki-oauth-global-token-revocation-06 Section 3.2](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-parecki-oauth-global-token-revocation-06#section-3.2): "This specification RECOMMENDS using a private key JWT as described in Section 3.5. Other authentication methods such as OAuth 2.0 Bearer Token [RFC6750] MAY be used where appropriate.") and `Bearer` is the challenge this refusal's branch selects for a request that is not a `Basic` attempt: the endpoint reads `Basic` in the `Authorization` field as a client authentication, answered in its own scheme above, and extracts a `Bearer` or a `DPoP` token there (`BearerTokenValidation.TryExtractAccessToken`).
    - A caller the revoke-subject seam answers `GlobalTokenRevocationOutcome.Forbidden` for is answered 403, the body's error `insufficient_scope`, and the challenge `Bearer realm="<issuer>", error="insufficient_scope"`. [RFC 6750 Section 3.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6750#section-3.1), `insufficient_scope`: "The request requires higher privileges than provided by the access token. The resource server SHOULD respond with the HTTP 403 (Forbidden) status code and MAY include the "scope" attribute with the scope necessary to access the protected resource." The endpoint names no scope: the decision is the application's and carries none. [draft-parecki-oauth-global-token-revocation-06 Section 3.4.2](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-parecki-oauth-global-token-revocation-06#section-3.4.2), 403 Forbidden: "Insufficient authorization, e.g. missing scopes."
    - A registration that is not a `ClientRecord` is answered 500 `server_error` with `WellKnownOAuthErrorDescriptions.ServerNotReady` and no challenge, since the fault is the server's wiring and not the caller's. An application's materializer that replaces the registration with a record of another type than the loaded one is answered so by the dispatcher before the endpoint runs (the materialized registration's entry above); the endpoint's own check answers the same for a registration that is not a `ClientRecord` when it runs. [RFC 9110 Section 15.6.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110#section-15.6.1): "The 500 (Internal Server Error) status code indicates that the server encountered an unexpected condition that prevented it from fulfilling the request."
    - A request the seam accepts is answered as the revoke-subject seam's outcome maps, and its answer carries no challenge.
  - **The application's turn.** The client authentication seam owns the method and is consulted for every request, one without an `Authorization` field included; the endpoint reads the one reading it took before the seam, to choose among the 401 shapes (`GlobalTokenRevocationEndpoints.RefuseUnauthenticatedAsync`). For a client whose registration names a Client ID Metadata Document, a reading that presents a credential is judged against the client's document: the materialization fetches it (the document of the request's `client_id`, else of the registration's own identifier) and the seam receives the record it materialized, and a failed fetch or a `client_id` naming another client is answered by that same body, so a caller cannot tell it from a refused authentication; a malformed reading, the empty line included, and a reading without a credential reach the endpoint as for any client (the client authentication entry below, its second class). A realm the application composes for its own challenges is derived the same way, as the remarks of `BearerTokenChallenge.BuildChallenge` state.
  - **Deviations.** Each is named with its reason.
    - An access token carried outside the `Authorization` field (for example as the URI query parameter of [RFC 6750 Section 2.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6750#section-2.3)) and refused by the seam is answered without an error code, where [RFC 6750 Section 3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6750#section-3) says: "If the protected resource request included an access token and failed authentication, the resource server SHOULD include the "error" attribute to provide the client with the reason why the access request was declined." The reason: the endpoint reads the `Authorization` field only.
  - The challenge is attached by `CredentialEndpointDpopValidation.Challenged`, the one body the client configuration endpoint's and the Shared Signals Transmitter's refusals are challenged through as well; the endpoint reaches it through `CredentialEndpointDpopValidation.ChallengedAsync`, which resolves the issuer once for the refusal.
  - Proven by `GlobalTokenRevocationServerTests` over the loopback listener: `ARevocationWhoseCredentialTheSeamRefusesIsAnInvalidToken` (401, `invalid_token`, the literal challenge), `ARevocationWithoutACredentialIsChallengedWithoutAnErrorCode` (401, an empty body, the literal challenge without `error`), `ARevocationWhoseAuthorizationFieldCarriesNoAccessTokenIsChallengedWithoutAnErrorCode` (an empty value and a value of white space: 401, an empty body, the literal challenge without `error`), `ARevocationWithARefusedBasicCredentialIsChallengedInTheBasicScheme` (401, `invalid_client`, the literal challenge `Basic realm="https://gtr.issuer.test/"`), `ARevocationWithTwoAuthorizationLinesIsAnInvalidRequest` and `ARevocationWhoseAuthorizationValueIsOutsideTheGrammarIsAnInvalidRequest` (a malformed credential: 400 `invalid_request`, the literal body, the literal challenge `Bearer realm="https://gtr.issuer.test/", error="invalid_request"`, the authentication seam not called, nothing revoked), `ACallerWithoutTheRightToRevokeTheSubjectIsInsufficientScope` (403, `insufficient_scope`, the literal challenge), `TheChallengeOfAnInternationalizedIssuerCarriesItsAsciiForm` (an issuer whose host holds U+00FC, the realm `https://xn--bcher-kva.example/`), `ARevocationServedUnderARegistrationTheEndpointCannotUseIsAServerError` (a materializer that answers a registration of another family: 500 `server_error`, no challenge), `ARefusalServedWhereNoIssuerResolvesIsAServerFault` (a refused request where no issuer resolves: the resolver's throw, answered by the host's HTTP application as 500 `server_error`, no challenge) and `ARevocationTheSeamAcceptsCarriesNoChallenge` (204, no `WWW-Authenticate` field); and in process by `GlobalTokenRevocationRejectsUnauthenticatedClient` (401, an empty body, the challenge without `error`, the body never parsed).
- **Per-session `sid` + RP-Initiated Logout, server side.** The ID Token carries a `sid` established at authorize (the app stamps `ExchangeContext.SetSessionId`, mirroring `SetAuthTime`/`SetSubjectId`; threaded authorize→`ServerCodeIssuedState`→`IssuanceContext`→`SidClaimContributor`) — per-authentication-session, not per-subject. The `end_session_endpoint` (`EndSessionEndpoints` in `Verifiable.OAuth.Logout`) verifies the `id_token_hint` as an AS-issued JWS via **`Jws.VerifyAndDecodeAsync`** (the payload comes from the VERIFIED result; signature + `iss` only, **`exp` deliberately not enforced** per RP-Initiated §3 — so it does not reuse `JwsAccessTokenValidator`), extracts `sub`/`sid`, validates `post_logout_redirect_uri` against the new `ClientRecord.AllowedPostLogoutRedirectUris`, drops out to `TerminateSessionDelegate`, and redirects 302 with `state` echoed (else 200). Fail-closed gate: capability + `TerminateSessionAsync` + `VerificationKeyResolver`. Federated/cross-server logout *propagation* is deferred to the Back-Channel (Slice 5) and CAEP-emit (Slice 7) slices. **Non-legacy logout params:** §3 `logout_hint` is supported as a *sessionless* path through a sibling seam `TerminateSessionByHintDelegate` (`Integration.TerminateSessionByHintAsync`) — when `id_token_hint` is absent but `logout_hint` is present and that seam is wired, the library passes the opaque hint verbatim (it does not interpret it; the app resolves it to a session) and runs the same redirect-URI validation + 302/200; with the seam unwired, `id_token_hint` remains required (fail-closed). A sibling seam, not an overload of `TerminateSessionDelegate`, because the verified-`sub`/`sid` path and the opaque-hint path are distinct non-nullable contracts. §3 `ui_locales` has no library action (the library renders no UI), so the only supported surface is the **`ui_locales_supported`** discovery advertisement, app-contributed via `ContributeDiscoveryFieldsAsync`; `end_session_endpoint` itself is library-emitted. `frontchannel_logout_supported`/`_session_supported` are correctly omitted (Front-Channel is legacy/skipped); `backchannel_logout_supported`/`_session_supported` arrive with Slice 5.
- **Back-Channel Logout, server side.** The Logout Token is a dedicated OAuth-layer primitive pair (`BackChannelLogout.BuildLogoutTokenAsync` / `VerifyLogoutTokenAsync` in `Verifiable.OAuth.Logout`), NOT a generic RFC 8417 SET: its `sub`/`sid` are top-level JWT claims (not an RFC 9493 `sub_id`) and its explicit `typ` is `logout+jwt` (`WellKnownMediaTypes.Jwt.LogoutJwt`), so it composes the JCose signing/verification path directly rather than reusing `SecurityEventTokenIssuance`/`Verification` (whose `typ` is `secevent+jwt`) — same crypto, different envelope. The verifier enforces the §2.6 rules (signature first, then `iss`/`aud`/`iat`, a `sub` and/or `sid`, the back-channel logout member in `events`, and **no `nonce`**) and returns a typed `BackChannelLogoutVerificationResult`. **There is no library orchestrator and no new OP endpoint** (the OP is the sender; the RP owns the `backchannel_logout_uri`): the fan-out IS the `DeliverBackChannelLogoutDelegate` seam (`Integration.DeliverBackChannelLogoutAsync`) the end-session endpoint drops out to *after* `TerminateSessionDelegate`, passing the verified `sub`/`sid`; the app enumerates its own session→RP store, builds a Logout Token per RP with the primitive, and delivers it. Behaviour and advertisement share one gate — capability `OidcBackChannelLogout` allowed **and** the deliver seam wired — so `backchannel_logout_supported`/`backchannel_logout_session_supported` are advertised iff the OP will actually fan out (the OP carries the per-session `sid`, hence session-supported too). `ClientRecord.BackchannelLogoutUri`/`BackchannelLogoutSessionRequired` mirror the registration. Cross-server *propagation* is proven by the firewalled multi-RP HTTP e2e `FederatedBackChannelLogoutHttpTests.FederatedLogoutPropagatesLogoutTokenToEveryRegisteredRpOverHttp` (Slice 5d): one OP `sid` is shared across two registered RPs (SSO), the OP signs a Logout Token per RP and POSTs it to each `ClientRecord.BackchannelLogoutUri` over a real Kestrel socket, and each RP reconstructs from the wire bytes plus the OP's published public key alone, verifies §2.6, and drops the session keyed by `sid`. The deliberate scope choice — a multi-RP firewalled HTTP propagation e2e rather than the full Federation trust-ring — is sound because Back-Channel Logout is OP→RP (OIDC), not a federation-trust-chain feature; the ring would add multi-host realism, not a dependency. The OP-side session setup and `end_session` trigger run in-process (already covered by `EndSessionLogoutTests.EndSessionFansOutBackChannelLogoutAfterTerminate`); the security-critical OP→RP `logout_token` delivery is what crosses the wire.
- **CAEP `session-revoked` emit, application-composed (Slice 7).** A revoked/terminated session MAY be signalled to Receivers as a best-effort CAEP `session-revoked` SET (CAEP 1.0 §3.1) — the "signal" end of the signal/command spectrum, distinct from the directed Back-Channel Logout fan-out. **There is deliberately no library issuance helper and no emit orchestrator:** the SET is composed from the primitives the application already holds — the typed `CaepSessionRevokedEvent.ToSecurityEvent()` carried by `SecurityEventTokenIssuance.IssueAsync`, with the revoked subject as the SET `sub_id` — inside the application's own seam (`RevokeSubjectTokensDelegate` for Global Token Revocation, or `TerminateSessionDelegate` for RP-Initiated Logout). A dedicated `CaepSessionRevokedSetIssuance` wrapper was considered and **rejected as thin sugar** over that one-line composition (which `CaepInteropEventTests` already exercises inline) — adding public surface without earning it. Every underlying piece was already covered on its own (the typed event + projection, SET issuance/reception, interop-profile conformance, the HTTP SET round-trip in `SsfHttpFlowTests`, and the documented app-driven hook in the GTR §8 decision); Slice 7 is the *flow-level* proof that ties them to the real revocation endpoint. `GlobalLogoutCaepEmitHttpTests.GlobalTokenRevocationEmitsConformantSessionRevokedSetToReceiverOverHttp`: a real `global_token_revocation_endpoint` request (returning §3 204) drops to the revoke-subject seam, which emits a CAEP-Interop-conformant (non-empty `reason_admin`) session-revoked SET about the revoked iss_sub subject and pushes it firewalled over Kestrel to a Receiver, which reconstructs from the wire bytes plus the OP's published public key alone and confirms the event type and `sub_id` through the full reception pipeline.
- **Shared Signals Transmitter, the Stream Management API's refusals and their challenges.** A request the application's `AuthorizeSsfRequestAsync` answers `SsfRequestDenialReason.AuthenticationRequired` for is answered 401 with one `Bearer` challenge whose `realm` is the issuer the request is served under. The general basis is the 401 row of the error tables of [Shared Signals Framework 1.0 Section 8.1.1.1](https://openid.net/specs/openid-sharedsignals-framework-1_0.html#section-8.1.1.1) and of the other Stream Management operations, "if authorization failed or it is missing"; [Section 8.1.2.1](https://openid.net/specs/openid-sharedsignals-framework-1_0.html#section-8.1.2.1) states it for reading a stream's status: "If a Receiver makes an unauthorized request, then the Transmitter MUST respond with a 401 error status." [RFC 9110 Section 15.5.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110#section-15.5.2): "The server generating a 401 response MUST send a WWW-Authenticate header field (Section 11.6.1) containing at least one challenge applicable to the target resource."
  - The transmitter's one challenge is `Bearer`, so it reads a Bearer token only. A request whose `Authorization` field holds a Bearer token, as `BearerTokenValidation.TryExtractBearer` reads it, is answered with the body's error `invalid_token`, the description "Authorization failed or is missing." (the wording of the 401 row of those error tables), and the challenge `Bearer realm="<issuer>", error="invalid_token"`. [RFC 6750 Section 3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6750#section-3): "If the protected resource request included an access token and failed authentication, the resource server SHOULD include the "error" attribute to provide the client with the reason why the access request was declined."
  - Every other such request (no `Authorization` field, an empty or white-space value, a credential of another scheme, a token presented under the `DPoP` scheme included) is answered with an empty body and the challenge `Bearer realm="<issuer>"`, which carries no error code. [RFC 6750 Section 3.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6750#section-3.1): "If the request lacks any authentication information (e.g., the client was unaware that authentication is necessary or attempted using an unsupported authentication method), the resource server SHOULD NOT include an error code or other error information."
  - A request the seam answers `SsfRequestDenialReason.InsufficientScope` for is answered 403 with the body's error `insufficient_scope` and the challenge `Bearer realm="<issuer>", error="insufficient_scope"`, as the Global Token Revocation endpoint's `Forbidden` is. [RFC 6750 Section 3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6750#section-3): "If the protected resource request does not include authentication credentials or does not contain an access token that enables access to the protected resource, the resource server MUST include the HTTP "WWW-Authenticate" response header field; it MAY include it in response to other conditions as well." [RFC 6750 Section 3.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6750#section-3.1), `insufficient_scope`: "The request requires higher privileges than provided by the access token. The resource server SHOULD respond with the HTTP 403 (Forbidden) status code and MAY include the "scope" attribute with the scope necessary to access the protected resource." The transmitter names no scope: the decision is the application's and carries none.
  - When the registration allows `OAuthProtectedResourceMetadata`, either challenge carries the `resource_metadata` URL of [RFC 9728 Section 5.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9728#section-5.1) after the realm and the error. The realm and that URL come from one resolution of the issuer per refusal, through `CredentialEndpointDpopValidation.ResolveIssuerAsync`, inside the refusal's guard, and the challenge is attached by `CredentialEndpointDpopValidation.Challenged`, the one body; the configuration document's `issuer` is read through the same resolution body.
  - No other answer of the transmitter carries a challenge.
  - **Deviations.** Each is named with its reason.
    - When no issuer resolves, the 401 and the 403 are answered without a `WWW-Authenticate` field, where [RFC 9110 Section 15.5.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110#section-15.5.2) says: "The server generating a 401 response MUST send a WWW-Authenticate header field (Section 11.6.1) containing at least one challenge applicable to the target resource." The reason: the realm is the issuer, and the transmitter keeps its answer for an issuer that does not resolve.
  - Proven by `SsfStreamManagementEndpointTests` over the listener: `AStreamManagementRequestWithoutACredentialIsChallengedWithoutAnErrorCode` (no `Authorization` field: 401, an empty body, the literal challenge without `error`), `AStreamManagementRequestWhoseAuthorizationFieldCarriesNoAccessTokenIsChallengedWithoutAnErrorCode` (an empty value, a value of white space and a `Basic` credential: the same answer), `AStreamManagementRequestPresentingATokenUnderTheDPoPSchemeIsChallengedWithoutAnErrorCode` (`DPoP forged-token`: the same answer), `AStreamManagementRequestWhoseAccessTokenIsRefusedIsAnInvalidToken` (401, `invalid_token`, the literal challenge with `error`), `ScopeEnforcementOverHttpWire` (a read-scoped token at Create Stream: 403, `insufficient_scope`, the literal challenge with `error`), `TheChallengeOfATransmitterServingItsResourceMetadataKeepsTheMetadataUrl` (the literal challenge with `error` and `resource_metadata`), `ARefusalOfTheTransmitterResolvesItsIssuerOnce` (the application's issuer resolver consulted once for the refusal, beside the dispatch's one resolution per request), `AStreamManagementRequestWithoutACredentialServedWhereNoIssuerResolvesIsUnchallenged`, `AStreamManagementRequestWhoseAccessTokenIsRefusedServedWhereNoIssuerResolvesIsUnchallenged` and `AStreamManagementRequestWhoseScopeIsInsufficientServedWhereNoIssuerResolvesIsUnchallenged` (a registration without an issuer and no issuer resolver: 401, or 403 `insufficient_scope`, without a `WWW-Authenticate` field, the deviation) and `AStreamManagementRequestTheSeamPermitsCarriesNoChallenge` (201, no `WWW-Authenticate` field).
- **RFC 8252 §7.3 loopback redirect exception, server side.** `RedirectUriMatching.IsRegisteredLoopback` is the narrow native-app companion to the exact-match matcher (`IsRegisteredExact`, which every call site tries first): a canonical-form gate first — `string.Equals(uri.OriginalString, uri.AbsoluteUri, Ordinal)` on both the requested URI and every registered candidate — refuses by construction the entire class of alternate-form bypasses (leading whitespace, non-canonical or default-re-stated ports, uppercase host casing, IPv4 shorthand/octal/decimal/hex encodings, longhand IPv6, and the bracketed-IPv6-plus-suffix trick where `Uri` folds a suffix into the path); only a candidate that survives it is compared on `Uri.Host` (against `127.0.0.1`, `[::1]`, and — only under the deployment opt-in `PolicyExchangeContextExtensions.IsLocalhostNameAcceptedForLoopbackRedirects`, default `false` per RFC 8252 §8.3's NOT RECOMMENDED — `localhost`), `Uri.IsLoopback`, `Uri.AbsolutePath`, and `Uri.Query`; the port is the one free variable RFC 8252 §7.3 grants, ignored on both the request and a ported registration alike. `AuthCodeEndpoints.IsAcceptableRedirectUri` gates the fallback to a public client (no declared `token_endpoint_auth_method`) presenting PKCE `S256` — the sole accepted PKCE method — at all three authorization-time sites (PAR, direct Authorize, JAR). The authorize redirect's `Location` is built from `Uri.OriginalString`, never `Uri.ToString()`, so the AS hands the client back exactly the string the token endpoint's RFC 6749 §4.1.3 check later demands: present → ordinal-equal to the code's persisted redirect URI or `invalid_grant`; absent → accepted, since this library's unconditional PKCE requirement means the OAuth 2.1 draft-16 §4.1.2.1 "MUST reject requests without a code_challenge from public clients" branch never applies here (§10.2 supplies only the "MUST allow"/"MUST enforce" redirect_uri-in-token-request rule, not the code_challenge MUST) — removing the unconditional redirect_uri check trades away a defence-in-depth layer RFC 9700 never credited to it: §4.4.2.1's actual AS-mix-up control is the RFC 9207 `iss` authorization-response parameter (or an ID Token `iss`), not PKCE and not an access token's `iss` claim. Proven by `RedirectUriMatchingTests`: Part C's canonical-form vector table (including the bracketed-IPv6 bypass vectors, the ":80"/uppercase-host canonical refusals, a ported-registration's own port being ignored, and a registered-candidate fragment being refused) drives the matcher directly; Part D drives the wired fallback in-process end to end; Part E drives every clause — any port (including a genuinely bound `[::1]`), the https/localhost/non-loopback refusals, the localhost opt-in, the direct-Authorize and JAR gates, and the RFC 6749 §4.1.3 / OAuth 2.1 §10.2 token-endpoint rule — over TestHostShell's real Kestrel-bound HTTPS loopback socket. RFC 6749 §3.1.2's "MUST be an absolute URI" / "MUST NOT include a fragment component" is enforced identically on both tiers: `IsRegisteredExact` and `IsRegisteredLoopback`'s canonical-form gate each refuse a relative or fragment-bearing candidate on either side (the requested value or a registered entry) as a permanent non-match, checking `IsAbsoluteUri` before any other `Uri` member is read so a relative candidate never throws.
- **RFC 9470 step-up authentication, server side (Slice 6).** Step-up enforcement converges on one authorize-decision seam: `Integration.EvaluateAuthorizationRequestAsync` (`EvaluateAuthorizationRequestDelegate`) is handed an `AuthorizationRequestEvaluation` (the requested `acr_values`/`max_age` and the session's `EstablishedAcr`/`auth_time`) and returns an `AuthorizationRequestDecision` — `Permit`, or `Deny(reason)` carrying an `AuthorizationDenialReason`. The library maps the reason to the OAuth error and error-redirect (`MapDenialReasonToError` in `AuthCodeEndpoints`: `UnmetAuthenticationRequirements` → `unmet_authentication_requirements` per OIDCUAR, `AccessDenied` → `access_denied`; a null/unmapped reason defaults to `access_denied`). The division of labour is **semantic decisions to the application, temporal to the library**: the app compares `acr` against its own authentication context (the library cannot know what an acr value *means*), while the library enforces `max_age` (OIDC Core §3.1.2.1) itself by whole-second comparison with **no clock skew** — `auth_time` and now share the AS clock, and any skew tolerance would defeat `max_age=0`. Every authorize path — direct, PAR, JAR — runs the same seam, so step-up cannot be bypassed by choice of request channel. The issued JWT access token carries `acr` + `auth_time` (RFC 9068 §2.2.1) when the host stamped them, and both survive refresh; `acr_values_supported` is advertised in discovery. Two front-channel hardenings rode the same slice: `state` is echoed on authorize **error** redirects (RFC 6749 §4.1.2.1), and PAR/JAR keep the pushed / `request_uri`-referenced value authoritative over a tampered front-channel duplicate (RFC 9126 + RFC 9101 §6.3), emitting an OTel span event when a tampering attempt is detected and ignored. Proven by `StepUpAccessTokenClaimsTests`, `UnmetAuthenticationRequirementsTests`, `AuthorizeStateEchoTests`, and `ParRequestIntegrityTests`.

- **The authentication instant is the host's, stated only as stamped.** [OpenID Connect Core 1.0 Section 2](https://openid.net/specs/openid-connect-core-1_0.html#IDToken) defines `auth_time` as "Time when the End-User authentication occurred." and states "When a max_age request is made or when auth_time is requested as an Essential Claim, then this Claim is REQUIRED; otherwise, its inclusion is OPTIONAL." The instant is a fact of the host's own authentication, so the library states none it was not given: every code-issuing authorize path (pushed, direct, and a signed request by value) carries the instant the host stamped (`ExchangeContext.SetAuthTime`) into `ServerAuthorizeCompleted.AuthTime` or `ServerDirectAuthorizeCompleted.AuthTime` and from there into `ServerCodeIssuedState.AuthTime` (each a `DateTimeOffset?`), `null` when the host stamped none, and the refresh chain carries what the code state holds (`ServerRefreshTokenIssuedState.AuthTime`). An unknown instant is carried as unknown and never becomes the instant the authorize leg runs. What follows from an unknown instant, as `AcrAmrClaimContributor` writes the claim: the access tokens of the code redemption and of every refresh, and every refreshed ID Token, carry no `auth_time`; the ID Token of the code redemption carries one only when the application's resolver (`ResolveOidcClaimsDelegate`) returns `AuthenticationContext.AuthTime`, which the contributor reads before the code's instant. A `max_age` request is refused with `unmet_authentication_requirements` before a code is issued (`EvaluateAuthenticationRequirementsAsync`), so the claim the clause makes REQUIRED for such a request is never left out of a token. For the Essential Claim case: the library does not read the `claims` request parameter and does not advertise `claims_parameter_supported`, so no Essential Claim request for `auth_time` reaches it; a host that needs `auth_time` on every ID Token stamps the instant (`ExchangeContext.SetAuthTime`). The library reads neither `require_auth_time` nor `default_max_age` of a client's registration metadata, so a host that registers such clients stamps the instant. Deviations: none. Proven by `AuthCodeParPkceRealWireFlowTests.ASessionStampedWithoutAnAuthenticationInstantIssuesNoAuthTime`, one case per start (over the real wire, the subject stamped and no instant: neither ID Token nor either access token of the redemption and the refresh carries `auth_time`; the pushed request and the pushed signed request reach the pushed authorize path, the direct request the direct one, and the signed request by value its own), `UnmetAuthenticationRequirementsTests.IdTokenOfAPermittedMaxAgeRequestCarriesTheStampedAuthTime` (a pin of [OpenID Connect Core 1.0 Section 3.1.2.1](https://openid.net/specs/openid-connect-core-1_0.html#AuthRequest), "When max_age is used, the ID Token returned MUST include an auth_time Claim Value.": a permitted `max_age` request with a stamped instant yields an ID Token whose `auth_time` is the stamp), `AcrAmrClaimContributorTests.EmitsAllThreeClaimsWhenAuthContextIsFullyPopulated` (the resolver's `AuthenticationContext.AuthTime` is written before the code's instant), `Oidc10IdTokenNonceAudienceScenarioTests.MatchingNonceValidatesAndSurfacesAuthenticationContext` (no stamped instant: the validated ID Token states no `auth_time`), and `Oidc10IdTokenProducerTests.PayloadCarriesExpectedClaimsForBaselineInput` (the whole baseline payload of an authentication without a stamped instant, which has no `auth_time` member); pinned by `Oidc10IdTokenNonceAudienceScenarioTests.AStampedAuthenticationInstantIsTheIdTokenAuthTime`, `RefreshedIdTokenAuthContextTests.RefreshedIdTokenPinsAuthTimeAndAcrToOriginalAuthentication` and `StepUpAccessTokenClaimsTests` (the stamped instant reaches the claim while the clock has moved past it), and by `UnmetAuthenticationRequirementsTests.MaxAgeWithoutEstablishedAuthTimeFailsClosed` (a `max_age` request without a stamped instant is refused and no code is issued).

- **Every ID Token the authorization server issues carries its own `jti`.** [RFC 7519 Section 4.1.7](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7519#section-4.1.7): "The "jti" (JWT ID) claim provides a unique identifier for the JWT." `Oidc10IdTokenProducer` writes a `jti` on every ID Token it builds, at the code redemption and at every refresh, drawn from the server's `GenerateIdentifierAsync` under `WellKnownIdentifierPurposes.OAuthJti`, the seam and purpose `Rfc9068AccessTokenProducer` draws the access token's `jti` from. A blank identifier is refused as the access token's is: a seam answer that is null, empty or white space only ends the token endpoint's walk with the `ArgumentException` that `JwtPayload.ForAccessToken` throws for the access token's, and no token of the walk is returned. The claim contributors the application wires (`AuthorizationServerIntegration.ClaimIssuer`) are merged into the payload after the producer has built it and can write any claim, `jti` included, since later contributions replace earlier values (`ContributionProfiles.StandardClaimIssuer`). Proven by `AuthCodeParPkceRealWireFlowTests.EveryIdTokenCarriesADistinctJti` (over the real wire, two code redemptions for one subject and one client and a refresh of the first, all at one instant: three non-empty `jti` values that differ), `Oidc10IdTokenProducerTests.IdTokenJtiIsTheIdentifierSeamsOwnDrawUnderTheJwtIdPurpose` (the ID Token's `jti` is the seam's answer to the producer's own draw, distinct from the access token's), and `Oidc10IdTokenProducerTests.ABlankJwtIdentifierForTheIdTokenIsRefusedAsForTheAccessToken` (over the real wire, the seam answers the access token's draw normally and the ID Token's with an empty and with a white space value: the code redemption is answered 500 with an empty body and no token, as for the same answer to the access token's draw).

- **The granted scope narrows at the authorize decision seam.** `AuthorizationRequestDecision.Permit(string? grantedScope)` optionally narrows the request's scope per [RFC 6749 §3.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6749#section-3.3): "The authorization server MAY fully or partially ignore the scope requested by the client." `AuthCodeEndpoints.EvaluateGrantedScope` validates the narrowed value is a non-empty subset of the requested tokens and canonicalizes it to the requested tokens' own order, deduplicated; a value outside the request or an empty grant is the application's own defect, answered `server_error` with `OAuthEventNames.SeamGrantedScopeExceedsRequest` recorded on the dispatch span rather than issued as a silent widening or an empty grant — an application that wants to grant nothing denies instead with `AuthorizationDenialReason.InvalidScope`. The redeemed token response's `scope` reflects the canonical value whenever it is set. Proven by `BarePermitGrantsTheRequestedScopeUnchanged`, `SeamPermitsWithNarrowerScopeRedeemsToATokenCarryingTheNarrowedScope`, `SeamPermittingAScopeOutsideTheRequestIsAServerErrorNotASilentWidening`, `SeamPermittingAnEmptyScopeIsAServerError`, `SeamGrantedScopeIsCanonicalizedToTheRequestOrderWithoutDuplicates`, and `DenyWithInvalidScopeReasonMapsToInvalidScopeErrorWithStateEchoed`.

- **Token-endpoint error taxonomy, server side.** `EndpointCandidate`/`ServerEndpoint` carry an optional `HandleNotFoundError`/`HandleNotFoundErrorDescription` pair an endpoint uses to name its own refusal when its correlation handle resolves to no live flow — unknown, expired, or already consumed — read by `EndpointServer.HandleCoreAsync` at every "handle not found" site; `null` falls back to the host-generic `invalid_request` vocabulary, correct for an endpoint whose handle carries no protocol-specific error semantics. The Authorization Code token endpoint sets the pair to `invalid_grant` (RFC 6749 §5.2: "the provided authorization grant ... is invalid, expired, revoked"). A token request missing `code` is still MATCHED by the endpoint — rather than falling through to the host's bodiless 404 no endpoint recognizing it would otherwise produce — so the correlation-key-resolution failure inside `HandleCoreAsync` answers with the OAuth-shaped `invalid_request` body §5.2 requires ("The request is missing a required parameter"). Proven by `AuthCodeParPkceRealWireFlowTests` (an unknown code, an expired code via the injected `FakeTimeProvider`, and a missing `code` parameter). A refusal reachable only after a caller has already proven possession of the grant's own secrets — the matching `code_verifier` digest at code redemption (RFC 7636 §4.6), or the bound client's identity at refresh (RFC 6749 §6) — keeps its own body instead of this constant, because it tells such a caller nothing about existence it did not already know: the `ResolveEffectiveResource` granted-set subset refusal (`invalid_target`, RFC 8707 §2.2), the `ResolveGrantedAuthorizationDetailsAsync` narrowing-beyond-grant refusal (`invalid_authorization_details`, RFC 9396 §6), and the application's own `ResolveCredentialAuthorizationAsync` denial all answer this way.
- **Client authentication: one reading per request, refused before any seam, challenged with its realm.** Every path that authenticates or identifies a client is one of three kinds. The authorization endpoint family identifies the client by `client_id` and authenticates none. The client-authenticating paths (the pushed authorization request and the pushed request carrying a request object, code redemption and its grant binding, refresh, client credentials, token exchange, the jwt-bearer grant, the pre-authorized code grant and its `invalid_client` denial, revocation, introspection, Global Token Revocation, and the Client ID Metadata Document materialization) read the request's credentials once, through `ClientCredentialReading.Read`, from the `Authorization` field and the `client_id`, `client_secret`, `client_assertion` and `client_assertion_type` form fields, before any seam runs, and read `client_id` through that reading (`ClientCredentialReading.ClientId`). The federation endpoints' gate authenticates a federation requester, not the tenant's OAuth client, through the application's seam, after the reading is taken and a malformed one refused. The document the materialization fetches is named per class: the reading's `client_id` at classes 2 to 4, the registration's own identifier as the fallback of classes 2 and 4, and at class 5 the `client_id` by the field's own rule. The reading is taken once and carried on the request's context to every later site (`AuthCodeEndpoints.CarriedClientCredentials`), so the endpoint reads the reading the materialization took. The materialization classifies the matched request once (`ClientIdMetadataMaterialization`, its private `Classify`), by the matched capability first, and one table says what each class does. (1) A protected resource (UserInfo; the OID4VCI Credential, Deferred Credential and Notification endpoints; the client configuration endpoint; the Shared Signals transmitter; and the resource side's endpoints that serve without a client: the OID4VCI Nonce, Credential Offer and Credential Issuer Metadata endpoints, the Protected Resource Metadata endpoint and the AuthZEN endpoints) is never judged: nothing is read, fetched or refused, whatever fields the request carries (a `client_id`, a `grant_type`, a malformed `Authorization` field), and the endpoint answers it as at a tenant without a metadata document. (2) Global Token Revocation authenticates the client ([draft-parecki-oauth-global-token-revocation-06 Section 3.2](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-parecki-oauth-global-token-revocation-06#section-3.2): "The request MUST also be authenticated."): a reading that presents a credential is judged against the document of its `client_id`, else of the registration's own identifier; a malformed reading, the empty line included, and a reading without a credential pass through and the endpoint answers them as it does; a `client_id` naming another client and a failed fetch are answered by the endpoint's own refusal of an unauthenticated request (`GlobalTokenRevocationEndpoints.RefuseUnauthenticatedAsync`, the body the endpoint answers a refused authentication with), so a caller cannot tell them from a refused authentication. (3) A pushed request, its request object form included, authenticates the client as the token endpoint does ([RFC 9126 Section 2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9126#section-2): "The rules for client authentication as defined in [RFC6749] for token endpoint requests, including the applicable authentication methods, apply for the PAR endpoint as well."): a malformed reading is refused before any fetch; the document fetched is the one its `client_id` names, and a request without one passes through to the endpoint, which answers "Missing client_id."; a `client_id` naming another client and a failed fetch are answered through `AuthCodeEndpoints.ClientAuthenticationFailureResponseAsync`, the body the endpoint's own refusal uses, one answer for both. (4) The token endpoint family (the capabilities that tag token-family endpoints alone: client credentials, token exchange, jwt-bearer, pre-authorized code, revocation, introspection, and the device authorization endpoint (`OAuthDeviceAuthorization`), which no builder of the library serves and an application's builder may, whose requests authenticate as the token endpoint's do ([RFC 8628 Section 3.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8628#section-3.1): "The client authentication requirements of Section 3.2.1 of [RFC6749] apply to requests on this endpoint, which means that confidential clients (those that have established client credentials) authenticate in the same manner as when making requests to the token endpoint, and public clients provide the "client_id" parameter to identify themselves."), every candidate under that capability being judged as that endpoint, while the endpoint of the user interaction ([RFC 8628 Section 3.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8628#section-3.3): "Authorization servers supporting this specification MUST implement a user-interaction sequence that starts with the user navigating to "verification_uri" and continues with them supplying the "user_code" at some stage during the interaction."), which a user agent reaches, carries a capability of the application's own; and any other capability outside the first three classes when the request carries a `grant_type` with a value, since the authorization-code capability tags the authorization endpoint and the token endpoint both): a malformed reading is refused before any fetch; the document fetched is the one its `client_id` names, else the registration's own, with a credential or with none, so every token-family request of such a tenant is judged against the client's document; a `client_id` naming another client and a failed fetch are answered through `ClientAuthenticationFailureResponseAsync`. (5) Every other request (the authorization endpoint family, end-session, discovery, the federation endpoints and the rest), whose endpoints of the library authenticate no client of the tenant: the reading's malformation is not judged there, the document fetched is the one its `client_id` names by the field's own rule alone (`ClientCredentialReading.ReadClientIdentifier`, the body `ClientCredentialReading.Read` reads every field with: one non-empty value; an empty occurrence is the omitted parameter; two non-empty values name no client), a request without one passes through, and a `client_id` naming another client and a failed fetch are answered 400 `invalid_request` with `RequestNotProcessed`. The federation endpoints' gate authenticates a federation requester through the application's seam (`AuthenticateFederationClientDelegate`), which is handed the registration as the dispatcher holds it: the stored one unless the request's `client_id` names the client and the document materialized it, or the request carries a `grant_type` with a value, which makes it a token request of the fourth class (the document fetched on the registration's own identifier, and a failed fetch refused as a client authentication failure). An application's own endpoint under a capability of its own is judged as a token request when its request carries a `grant_type` with a value, and otherwise by `client_id` alone, so one that authenticates the client on a request carrying neither is served on the stored registration. The order matters: a protected resource request that carries a `grant_type` is a protected resource request. The invariant, bounded to the endpoints the library builds: whenever such an endpoint identifies or authenticates the client of a tenant whose registration names a metadata document, the record it judges is the document's; a request the materialization does not judge is answered by its endpoint as at a tenant without a metadata document. The federation gate's requester and an application's endpoint under a capability of its own lie outside that bound and are judged as the fifth class says. The public `ClientIdMetadataMaterialization.IsTokenEndpointRequest` answers true exactly for the fourth class. The federation endpoints keep their own seam (`AuthenticateFederationClientDelegate`), which receives the request's fields and not the reading; the reading is taken and a malformed one refused before it runs. The `Authorization` field is read through `AuthorizationFieldReading.Read` (in `Verifiable.Server`), the one reader of the field the bearer resources and the client configuration endpoint share: the field is absent, repeated, or one field line with its scheme, its credentials, whether its value matches the grammar and whether its credentials are a token68 (`HasToken68Credentials`), the scheme compared ASCII case-insensitively. The reading's text names the scheme of a well-formed line only, and of any other line a fixed word, since the text before the first SP of a line outside the grammar may hold the credential itself.
  - **The grammar and the repeat.** [RFC 9110 Section 11.6.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110#section-11.6.2): "Authorization = credentials", one credential and no list, so a second field line is what [RFC 9110 Section 5.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110#section-5.3) forbids: "This means that, aside from the well-known exception noted below, a sender MUST NOT generate multiple field lines with the same name in a message (whether in the headers or trailers) or append a field line when a field line of the same name already exists in the message, unless that field's definition allows multiple field line values to be recombined as a comma-separated list (i.e., at least one alternative of the field's definition allows a comma-separated list, such as an ABNF rule of #(values) defined in Section 5.6.1)." One line is judged by [RFC 9110 Section 11.4](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110#section-11.4), "credentials = auth-scheme [ 1*SP ( token68 / #auth-param ) ]", with "auth-scheme    = token" and the scheme compared as [Section 11.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110#section-11.1) says: "It uses a case-insensitive token to identify the authentication scheme:", followed by that rule. A present field line that is empty or holds white space only has no scheme token and is outside the grammar like any other such value: one grammar reading with no exception, since the rule of [RFC 6749 Section 3.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6749#section-3.2) for a value sent empty governs request parameters, not header fields. A scheme longer than 20 characters is read as outside the grammar, a policy of the library: the scheme of a refused attempt is written back in its challenge, so the reading bounds it, and the longest name in the [IANA HTTP Authentication Scheme Registry](https://www.iana.org/assignments/http-authschemes/http-authschemes.xhtml) is `SCRAM-SHA-256`, 13 characters. A list of auth-param may start with an empty element and OWS before its comma, as the recipient rule of [RFC 9110 Section 5.6.1.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110#section-5.6.1.2) accepts ("#element => [ element ] *( OWS "," OWS [ element ] )"); the SP after the scheme stays required.
  - **The refusals.** A malformed reading is answered 400 `invalid_request` with one of three fixed descriptions of `WellKnownOAuthErrorDescriptions`, and no seam runs: a client authentication form field carrying two non-empty values (`RepeatedClientAuthenticationParameter`; [RFC 6749 Section 3.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6749#section-3.2): "Request and response parameters MUST NOT be included more than once."), the `Authorization` field on two field lines or outside the grammar (`MalformedAuthorizationField`), or more than one mechanism among a `Basic` field, a field of another scheme, a form secret and a form assertion (`MoreThanOneClientAuthenticationMethod`; [RFC 6749 Section 2.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6749#section-2.3): "The client MUST NOT use more than one authentication method in each request."). [RFC 6749 Section 5.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6749#section-5.2), `invalid_request`: "The request is missing a required parameter, includes an unsupported parameter value (other than grant type), repeats a parameter, includes multiple credentials, utilizes more than one mechanism for authenticating the client, or is otherwise malformed." An empty value is the omitted parameter, so an empty occurrence beside a valued one is not a repeat: "Parameters sent without a value MUST be treated as if they were omitted from the request." (Section 3.2). That holds for `client_id` as for the credentials: `client_id=` beside `client_id=<x>` names the client `<x>` at every site, and two non-empty values are a repeated parameter. The pre-authorized code grant refuses a malformed reading before its code-validation seam runs, so a malformed request never consumes the code.
  - **What the seam receives.** `ValidateClientCredentialsDelegate` takes the `ClientCredentialReading` in place of the request and its form fields: the seam judges the one credential the library read (`HasBasicAuthorization` with the field's credentials, `ClientSecret`, `ClientAssertion` with `ClientAssertionType`, or `HasOtherAuthorization`), never a credential the library refused or one value of a field sent twice. `PrivateKeyJwtClientAuthentication` reads the assertion and its type from the reading. A non-credential field a deployment's policy reads (a vendor parameter, the requested scope) is on the request the context carries; a seam that judges credentials reads them from the reading only, never from the request, whose raw fields hold every value of a field sent twice and an empty occurrence beside a valued one.
  - **The challenges.** A failed authentication is answered `invalid_client` by one body, `AuthCodeEndpoints.ClientAuthenticationRefusal`. A request that attempted the `Authorization` field is answered 401 with the challenge of its scheme, [RFC 6749 Section 5.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6749#section-5.2): "If the client attempted to authenticate via the "Authorization" request header field, the authorization server MUST respond with an HTTP 401 (Unauthorized) status code and include the "WWW-Authenticate" response header field matching the authentication scheme used by the client." The challenge names its realm, [RFC 7617 Section 2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7617#section-2): "The authentication parameter 'realm' is REQUIRED ([RFC7235], Section 2.2).", the realm the header-safe form of the issuer the request is served under (`CredentialEndpointDpopValidation.HeaderSafeIssuer`, the body every challenge of the library writes its realm through): `Basic realm="<issuer>"`. A failed attempt of another scheme is answered 401 with the challenge of that scheme and, when `ClientAuthenticationMethodsSupported` advertises `client_secret_basic`, the `Basic` challenge after it in the same field, `<scheme> realm="<issuer>", Basic realm="<issuer>"` ([RFC 9110 Section 11.6.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110#section-11.6.1), "WWW-Authenticate = #challenge"), so the answer matches the client's scheme and names one the server applies. Any other failed request is answered 401 with the `Basic` challenge when `client_secret_basic` is advertised ("The authorization server MAY return an HTTP 401 (Unauthorized) status code to indicate which HTTP authentication schemes are supported."), and otherwise 400, the section's default status ("The authorization server responds with an HTTP 400 (Bad Request) status code (unless specified otherwise) and includes the following parameters with the response:"), so no 401 leaves without a challenge: [RFC 9110 Section 15.5.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110#section-15.5.2), "The server generating a 401 response MUST send a WWW-Authenticate header field (Section 11.6.1) containing at least one challenge applicable to the target resource." The materialization's refusal (`ClientIdMetadataMaterialization.Refuse`), the pre-authorized code grant's `invalid_client` denial and the federation endpoints' client authentication gate answer through the same bodies. The materialization's own refusals of a pushed request and a token request (a client identifier mismatch, a failed fetch) answer through `AuthCodeEndpoints.ClientAuthenticationFailureResponseAsync`, so their realm is the issuer the token endpoint resolves, through the application's resolver when one is wired and the library's default resolution (`DefaultIssuerResolver`, shape-checked by `IssuerIdentifierValidation.IsValidIssuerShape`) otherwise; an issuer that does not resolve, a resolver that answers none included, leaves no realm and a 400 with no challenge, whatever issuer the context carries. The public `ClientIdMetadataMaterialization.Refuse`, which an application rule composes synchronously, shapes a pushed request and a token request as the client authentication failure and every other request as 400 `RequestNotProcessed`; its realm, with the application's resolver wired, is the context's issuer as the dispatcher left it, a value an earlier step placed on the context before a resolution the resolver declined included, and, with no resolver wired, the value of the library's default resolution. So with a resolver that declines and a preset issuer, `Refuse` names the preset realm where the library's own refusal names none (making `Refuse` read nothing after a declined resolution needs the dispatcher to mark the declination). A request served under a registration of another family than `ClientRecord`, which only an application's materializer can place, is answered 500 `server_error` with `ServerNotReady` and no challenge (`AuthCodeEndpoints.UnresolvedRegistrationResponse`), as Global Token Revocation answers it: the fault is the server's wiring.
  - **The bearer resources.** UserInfo, the OID4VCI resources and the client configuration endpoint read the field once through `BearerTokenValidation.ReadAuthorizationField`; a field on two lines is answered 400 `invalid_request` (`CredentialEndpointDpopValidation.RefuseRepeatedAuthorizationAsync`; [RFC 6750 Section 3.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6750#section-3.1): "The request is missing a required parameter, includes an unsupported parameter or parameter value, repeats the same parameter, uses more than one method for including an access token, or is otherwise malformed.  The resource server SHOULD respond with the HTTP 400 (Bad Request) status code."). UserInfo and the Credential, Deferred Credential and Notification endpoints, which accept the `DPoP` scheme, carry the `Bearer` and the `DPoP` challenge, each with the error, `Bearer realm="<issuer>", error="invalid_request", DPoP error="invalid_request", algs="…"`, the shape [RFC 9449 Section 7.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-7.2)'s Figure 19 shows ("Otherwise, both Bearer and DPoP challenges MAY be used to deliver error information (Figure 19)."); the client configuration endpoint, which reads a Bearer token only, carries `Bearer realm="<issuer>", error="invalid_request"`. A scheme written `bearer` is the `Bearer` scheme. The token is taken only from a well-formed line whose credentials are a token68, the form "credentials = "Bearer" 1*SP b64token" ([RFC 6750 Section 2.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6750#section-2.1)) and "credentials = "DPoP" 1*SP token68" ([RFC 9449 Section 7.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-7.1)) define; a line whose credentials are a list of auth-param carries no token and is answered as a request without one, 401 with the challenge and no error code. At these resources and at Global Token Revocation a present line that is empty or white space only is a request that lacks the authentication information the endpoint reads, answered 401 without an error code ([RFC 6750 Section 3.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6750#section-3.1): "If the request lacks any authentication information (e.g., the client was unaware that authentication is necessary or attempted using an unsupported authentication method), the resource server SHOULD NOT include an error code or other error information."); at every client-authenticating site of the token endpoint family it is the malformed field, 400 `invalid_request` with `MalformedAuthorizationField`. Global Token Revocation hands the reading of such a line on to its client authentication seam, marked `IsAuthorizationMalformed` and naming no mechanism. A Client ID Metadata Document client is answered the same way at these resources, since the materialization never judges a protected resource request, and at Global Token Revocation, since a reading without a credential passes through to the endpoint.
