Proven by `GlobalTokenRevocationServerTests.ARevocationWhoseAuthorizationFieldCarriesNoAccessTokenIsChallengedWithoutAnErrorCode` (an empty and a white-space line, a refusing seam: 401, an empty body, `Bearer realm="https://gtr.issuer.test/"`; without the endpoint's exception for such a line the answer is 400 `invalid_request`, `MalformedAuthorizationField` and `Bearer realm="https://gtr.issuer.test/", error="invalid_request"`), the pin `GlobalTokenRevocationServerTests.AnAuthorizationLineWithoutACredentialReachesTheSeamAsAReadingWithoutCredentials` (an accepting seam receives `HasCredentials` false, `IsAuthorizationMalformed` true and `ClientId` null), `DynamicRegistrationEndToEndTests.AClientConfigurationRequestWhoseAuthorizationFieldCarriesNoBearerTokenIsChallenged` (the client configuration endpoint: 401 with the challenge), `GlobalTokenRevocationServerTests.AnEmptyAuthorizationLineOfAMetadataDocumentClientIsChallengedWithoutAnErrorCode`, `ClientIdMetadataDocumentAdversarialFlowTests.AnEmptyAuthorizationLineAtUserInfoIsChallengedWithoutAnErrorCodeAsync` and `ClientIdMetadataDocumentAdversarialFlowTests.TwoAuthorizationLinesAtUserInfoCarryBothChallengesAsync` (a Client ID Metadata Document client: 401 with the challenge and no error code, and 400 with both challenges), and `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.AnEmptyAuthorizationLineBesideAValidSecretIsAnInvalidRequest` (the token endpoint: 400 `invalid_request`).
  - **Deviations.** Each is named with its reason.
    - [RFC 7662 Section 2.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7662#section-2.3) says "If the protected resource uses OAuth 2.0 client credentials to authenticate to the introspection endpoint and its credentials are invalid, the authorization server responds with an HTTP 401 (Unauthorized) as described in Section 5.2 of OAuth 2.0 [RFC6749]." At a server that advertises no `client_secret_basic`, a failed form authentication at introspection is answered 400. The reason: such a server has no challenge to send, and a 401 without one breaks RFC 9110 Section 15.5.2. Proven by `TokenIntrospectionServerTests.IntrospectionEndpointRejectsUnauthenticatedCallerWithoutTouchingTheStore` (no credential: 400 `invalid_client`, no `WWW-Authenticate`) and the pin `TokenIntrospectionServerTests.AWrongFormSecretAtAServerWithoutBasicIsABadRequestWithoutAChallenge` (a wrong `client_secret` over the wire: 400 `invalid_client`, the literal body, no `WWW-Authenticate`).
    - A request whose issuer does not resolve has no realm, and its failed authentication is answered 400 with no challenge, also when it attempted the `Authorization` field, where [RFC 6749 Section 5.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6749#section-5.2) says: "If the client attempted to authenticate via the "Authorization" request header field, the authorization server MUST respond with an HTTP 401 (Unauthorized) status code and include the "WWW-Authenticate" response header field matching the authentication scheme used by the client." The issuer does not resolve when no application resolver is wired and neither the registration nor the context names an issuer of the issuer shape, and when the application's resolver answers none, also while the context holds an issuer an earlier step placed there, which the token endpoint's resolution does not use. The reason: a challenge without a realm breaks RFC 7617 Section 2, and a 401 without a challenge breaks RFC 9110 Section 15.5.2. Proven by the pin `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.AFailedBasicAttemptWhereNoIssuerResolvesIsABadRequestWithoutAChallenge` (no issuer resolver, no issuer on the registration or the context, a wrong secret in `Authorization: Basic`: 400 `invalid_client`, no `WWW-Authenticate`), `ClientIdMetadataDocumentAdversarialFlowTests.AFailedDocumentFetchUnderAnIssuerOutsideTheIssuerShapeIsABadRequestWithoutAChallengeAsync` (the materialization's refusal of a failed fetch with `Authorization: Basic`, no resolver, a registration issuer of the `http` scheme: 400 `invalid_client`, no `WWW-Authenticate`) and `ClientIdMetadataDocumentAdversarialFlowTests.AFailedDocumentFetchWhereTheIssuerResolverAnswersNoneIsABadRequestWithoutAChallengeAsync` (the same where the resolver answers none and the context carries a preset issuer: 400 `invalid_client`, no `WWW-Authenticate`).
    - [OpenID Federation 1.0 Section 8.9](https://openid.net/specs/openid-federation-1_0.html#section-8.9) defines `invalid_client` as "The Client cannot be authorized or is not a valid participant of the federation." and continues "The HTTP response status code SHOULD be 401 (Unauthorized)." A failed client authentication at a federation endpoint that did not use the `Authorization` field, at a server that advertises no `client_secret_basic`, is answered 400. The reason: such a server has no challenge applicable to the endpoint, and a 401 without one breaks RFC 9110 Section 15.5.2. Proven by `FederationFetchEndpointTests.FetchEndpointRejectsWhenClientAuthenticationFails`.
    - [RFC 9449 Section 7.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-7.2) recommends: "If the mechanism used to attempt authentication could be established unambiguously, then the corresponding challenge SHOULD be used to deliver error information (Figure 18)." A repeated `Authorization` field at a resource that accepts `DPoP` is answered with both challenges also when every line names one scheme. The reason: the reading of a repeated field keeps no scheme, so the library does not judge which line is the attempt. Proven by `UserInfoEndpointTests.UserInfoRefusesTwoAuthorizationLinesWithBothChallengesAsync`, `Oid4VciDpopCredentialEndpointTests.CredentialEndpointRefusesTwoAuthorizationLinesWithBothChallengesAsync`, the Deferred Credential and Notification rows of `Oid4VciDeferredAndNotificationTests.ProtectedEndpointRefusesTwoAuthorizationLinesWithBothChallengesAsync` and `ClientIdMetadataDocumentAdversarialFlowTests.TwoAuthorizationLinesAtUserInfoCarryBothChallengesAsync` (two `Authorization: Bearer` lines sent by `RawHttpsRequest`: 400 `invalid_request` and the literal field with both challenges).
  - Proven by, over the loopback listener: `AuthCodeClientAuthenticationTests.ARepeatedClientAssertionIsAnInvalidRequest` (400 `invalid_request`, the literal body), `AuthCodeClientAuthenticationTests.TwoAuthorizationLinesAreAnInvalidRequest` (two `Authorization: Basic` field lines sent by `RawHttpsRequest`: 400 `invalid_request`), `ClientCredentialReadingTests`.
- **Authorization-code lifecycle: hash at rest, claims, replay and reuse.** Authorization redirects carry the raw code; `ServerCodeIssuedState` persists its SHA-256 base64url `CodeHash`. Token correlation hashes the presented code, so the stored hash is not accepted as the wire code. `BeforeCodeRedemptionCorrelationAsync`, the endpoint's pre-correlation step, runs identification, issuer resolution, the `code_verifier`'s presence and grammar, declared client authentication, the request-only `client_id` rule, the request-only `resource` and `authorization_details` decisions, and DPoP — all before the presented code is ever looked up. `VerifyCodeGrantPresentation` then runs the checks that need the loaded code state — the PKCE digest, the grant-binding comparison, and the `redirect_uri` comparison — before claiming the loaded flow step and minting. PAR authorization claims its request URI in the same way. A valid code replay re-verifies through `VerifyCodeGrantPresentation`, revokes the original issuance audit, then walks the refresh family rooted at `ServerTokenIssuedState.RefreshFlowId` through retired links to the live token. It records `RevokedAt` through the pure `ServerAuthorizationCodeReplayDetected` transition only after that work; sequential repeats skip revocation. Invalid code presentations revoke nothing (OAuth 2.1 draft-16 §7.5.3: "The authorization server SHOULD NOT revoke any issued tokens when receiving a replayed authorization code that contains invalid parameters"). The Pre-Authorized Code grant's own stepless `authorization_details` resolution no longer runs the OID4VCI `locations` decision (and with it issuer resolution and Credential Issuer metadata contribution) when the grant carries `authorization_details` but the token request does not — that decision was already unread on this path; no wire answer changes.

- **Refresh rotation and reuse.** `BeforeRefreshCorrelationAsync`, the endpoint's pre-correlation step, carries identification, issuer resolution, declared client authentication, and the request-only `client_id` rule — OAuth 2.1 draft-16 §4.3.1's stored-grant binding rule ("if client authentication is included in the request, ensure that the refresh token was issued to the authenticated client, OR if a client_id is included in the request, ensure the refresh token was issued to the matching client") read fail-closed on neither identity (`invalid_request`). `VerifyRefreshClient` then keeps only the record-dependent half, once the record is loaded: the bound-client comparison. Validated credentials identify the registration; without credentials the form must identify the client. Confidential clients must authenticate. Both the live and reuse paths call `DpopTokenEndpointValidation.BindValidatedProofAsync`, reading the outcome the step's own `ValidatePresentedProofAsync` call already carried, and require a matching proof for a bound token. Rotation claims the loaded step before minting, side-saves a fresh refresh state, and retires the presented record with `SuccessorRefreshFlowId`. Its `ExpiresAt` is the maximum of issued-token expiry and successor refresh expiry. `PredecessorFlowId` on each live refresh state names the flow that audited its paired access token: the code flow initially, the presented refresh flow at rotation. The retirement transition preserves that backlink.

- **Every token-issuing grant applies the registration profile's DPoP mandate.** The authorization-code and refresh grants no longer call `DpopTokenEndpointValidation.ValidateAsync` at all: each instead runs the request-only half of DPoP in its own pre-correlation step (`ValidatePresentedProofAsync`, with `proofRequiredByRegistration = ClientPolicyProfiles.RequiresDpop(registration.Profile)` — the refresh grant's request-only half derives from the REGISTRATION's profile here, exactly like every other grant, so a DPoP-mandating profile presenting any refresh record without a proof is challenged), and the record's own `cnf.jkt` binding is applied afterwards, in the handler, by `BindValidatedProofAsync`. `ValidateAsync` itself runs with `dpopRequired = ClientPolicyProfiles.RequiresDpop(registration.Profile)` at its four remaining callers — the stepless grants without a pre-correlation step: Pre-Authorized Code, `client_credentials`, token exchange, and JWT Bearer. `RequiresDpop` returns `true` under `PolicyProfile.Fapi20` — [FAPI 2.0 Security Profile §5.3.2.1](https://openid.net/specs/fapi-security-profile-2_0-final.html#section-5.3.2.1): the authorization server "shall only issue sender-constrained access tokens" — and under `PolicyProfile.Haip10`, whose [§4](https://openid.net/specs/openid4vc-high-assurance-interoperability-profile-1_0-final.html#section-4) requires DPoP support of Wallets and Credential Issuers and which the library treats as the same mandate. Under either, a request presenting no proof is answered with a `use_dpop_nonce` challenge carrying a `DPoP-Nonce` header, and a proof without the server's nonce is challenged once. Every grant runs the validation before its first step that can consume a single-use artifact — the authorization code's and the refresh token's claim, the pre-authorized code seam, the JWT Bearer assertion seam and the assertion's `jti` record, the token exchange's subject-token, actor-token and authorization seams — because the retry [RFC 9449 §8](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-8) prescribes presents the same request: a challenge or a rejected proof must not burn the artifact (`RedeemCompletesAfterOneNonceRetryOverRealWireUnderMandatingRegistration`, `RedeemWithInvalidDpopProofDoesNotConsumeTheAssertion`, `RegistrationRequiringDpopChallengeDoesNotConsultTheSubjectSeamBeforeTheRetry`; the replay defense after a complete redemption stays pinned by `RedeemRejectsReplayedJti`). A presented proof binds the token (`IssuanceContext.Confirmation`, `cnf.jkt`, `token_type=DPoP` per [RFC 9449 §5](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-5)). On the token exchange and JWT Bearer grants, `IdJagDpopDecision.Evaluate` runs its proof-of-possession matrix where its inputs exist, after the seams, reading the outcome of the validation that ran before them and combining the presented proof's key thumbprint with the grant's own bound thumbprint and the Resource Server's sender-constraint requirement. Proven by `TokenExchangeGrantTests.RegistrationRequiringDpopWithoutAProofIsRefused`, `JwtBearerGrantTests.RegistrationRequiringDpopWithoutAProofIsRefused`, `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.RegistrationRequiringDpopWithoutAProofIsRefused`, `Oid4VciPreAuthorizedCodeGrantTests.RegistrationRequiringDpopWithoutAProofIsRejectedWithoutConsultingTheSeam`, `DpopEndToEndTests.TokenIssuanceAndResourceCallValidateUnderDpopProtocol`, and the refresh grant's own `DpopBoundRefreshTests.DpopSenderConstrainedRefreshTokenRefreshesThroughRealClientAsync` / `NonDpopClientStillRefreshesThroughRealClientAsync` / `DpopMandatingProfileChallengesAnUnboundRefreshRecordObtainedBeforeTheProfileMandatedItAsync` (a registration-profile alteration to a DPoP-mandating profile, after a Bearer refresh record already exists, is challenged on the record's own profile, not its stored binding).

- **DPoP delegate wiring is all-or-nothing, checked wherever a DPoP decision can be reached.** `DpopTokenEndpointValidation`'s three entry points — `ValidateAsync`, `ValidatePresentedProofAsync`, and `BindValidatedProofAsync`'s bound-record/no-proof branch — each check the SAME five delegates (`ValidateDpopProofAsync`, `IssueDpopNonceAsync`, `ValidateDpopNonceAsync`, `ResolveServerHmacKeyAsync`, `GetHmacKeySetAsync`) before issuing a nonce challenge or accepting a proof, answering `server_error` when any one is missing rather than issuing a nonce the server could never validate a retried proof against, or silently skipping a check a partial wiring left out. Proven by `DpopBoundRefreshTests.BoundRefreshWithoutAProofAndPartiallyWiredDpopFailsClosedAsync`.

- **The Pre-Authorized Code grant validates DPoP before the code seam.** `AuthCodeEndpoints.BuildPreAuthorizedCodeToken` runs `DpopTokenEndpointValidation.ValidateAsync` before `AuthorizationServerIntegration.ValidatePreAuthorizedCodeAsync`, because the pre-authorized code is single-use and the seam may consume it: a `use_dpop_nonce` challenge or a rejected proof must not consume it, so the Wallet retries the same code once it satisfies the challenge. A presented proof's confirmation rides the DPoP outcome's `Confirmation` into `IssuanceContext.Confirmation`, and the response `token_type` follows it. Proven by `NonceChallengeRunsBeforeTheSeamAndTheRetryWithTheSameCodeSucceeds`, `PresentedProofBindsTheAccessTokenAndAnswersDpopTokenType`, `LongLivedAccessTokenIsIssuedWithAProofAndRefusedWithoutOne`, `ProofWithWrongHtuIsRejectedWithoutConsultingTheSeam`, `ProofWithWrongHtmIsRejectedWithoutConsultingTheSeam`, and `ReplayedJtiIsRejectedWithoutConsultingTheSeamASecondTime`. Under a profile requiring an access-token audience this grant has no [RFC 8707 §2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8707#section-2) `resource` parameter and no scope-derived `ScopeToAudience` entry of its own; an application that mandates an audience under this grant wires `AuthorizationServerIntegration.ResolveAccessTokenAudienceAsync` itself.

- **A Credential Request is held to the Credential Issuer catalogue and to what its Access Token granted.** The Credential Endpoint materializes only when `AuthorizationServerIntegration.ContributeCredentialIssuerMetadataAsync` is wired beside the request parser and the issuance seam, as the Credential Issuer Metadata endpoint does, so no request is served against an unknown catalogue ([OID4VCI 1.0 Section 12.2.4](https://openid.net/specs/openid-4-verifiable-credential-issuance-1_0.html#section-12.2.4): "credential_configurations_supported: REQUIRED. Object that describes specifics of the Credential that the Credential Issuer supports issuance of."). The catalogue is read once per request and that one answer is handed to every rule that reads it (the encryption requirements, the scope binding, the key attestation and the batch ceiling), so a catalogue the application alters while a request is served judges the request once. The Deferred Credential Endpoint requires the same seam beside `ResolveDeferredCredentialAsync`, and at both endpoints a request asking for an encrypted response from a catalogue advertising no `credential_response_encryption` is refused `invalid_encryption_parameters` with a fixed description ([OID4VCI 1.0 Section 8.2](https://openid.net/specs/openid-4-verifiable-credential-issuance-1_0.html#section-8.2): "The Credential Issuer indicates support for encrypted responses by including the credential_response_encryption parameter in the Credential Issuer Metadata." [Section 8.3.1.2](https://openid.net/specs/openid-4-verifiable-credential-issuance-1_0.html#section-8.3.1.2): "invalid_encryption_parameters: This error occurs when the encryption parameters in the Credential Request are either invalid or missing."). Both endpoints judge a request only against a catalogue the Credential Issuer Metadata endpoint publishes: right after the one read, the catalogue passes `CredentialIssuerMetadataValidation.Validate`, the one body that decides what that endpoint publishes, and a catalogue it refuses (a configuration without `format`, a proof type without `proof_signing_alg_values_supported`, a `batch_size` below 2, an encryption object lacking a REQUIRED member or carrying `encryption_required` as anything but a boolean, a malformed `display` entry) is answered exactly as that endpoint answers it, 500 `server_error` with the same body, before any rule reads it and with neither the issuance nor the deferred seam reached: the request is the server's own fault ([OID4VCI 1.0 Section 12.2.4](https://openid.net/specs/openid-4-verifiable-credential-issuance-1_0.html#section-12.2.4), on `credential_response_encryption`: "encryption_required: REQUIRED. Boolean value specifying whether the Credential Issuer requires the additional encryption on top of TLS for the Credential Response." [RFC 9110 Section 15.6.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110#section-15.6.1): "The 500 (Internal Server Error) status code indicates that the server encountered an unexpected condition that prevented it from fulfilling the request."). A `credential_response_encryption` object the catalogue carries therefore holds a non-empty `alg_values_supported` and a non-empty `enc_values_supported`, the two lists the object makes REQUIRED, and the requested `alg` and `enc` are always checked against both lists ([OID4VCI 1.0 Section 12.2.4](https://openid.net/specs/openid-4-verifiable-credential-issuance-1_0.html#section-12.2.4): "alg_values_supported: REQUIRED. A non-empty array containing a list of the JWE [RFC7516] encryption algorithms (alg values) [RFC7518] supported by the Credential Endpoint to encode the Credential Response in a JWT [RFC7519]." And: "enc_values_supported: REQUIRED. A non-empty array containing a list of the JWE [RFC7516] encryption algorithms (enc values) [RFC7518] supported by the Credential Endpoint to encode the Credential Response in a JWT [RFC7519]."). A catalogue whose object sets `encryption_required` and carries no lists issues no Credential: every request under it is answered 500, whether it asks for an encrypted response or not.
  - **The grant rules.** After the exclusivity check, `Oid4VciEndpoints.AuthorizeCredentialRequest` reads the `openid_credential` objects the token endpoint embedded in the token's `authorization_details` claim. With such objects, a request by `credential_configuration_id` is refused `invalid_credential_request`, and a `credential_identifier` no object lists is refused `unknown_credential_identifier`; without them, a request by `credential_identifier` is refused `invalid_credential_request`. An identifier the grant lists under more than one configuration names no one Credential Dataset; the grant is the application's inconsistency, so the request is answered 500 `server_error` with a fixed description and the issuance seam is not consulted; an identifier is ambiguous only across distinct configurations, so one listed in two objects naming the same configuration is served ([OID4VCI 1.0 Section 6.2](https://openid.net/specs/openid-4-verifiable-credential-issuance-1_0.html#section-6.2): "credential_identifiers: REQUIRED. A non-empty array of strings, each uniquely identifying a Credential Dataset that can be issued using the Access Token returned in this response."). [OID4VCI 1.0 Section 8.2](https://openid.net/specs/openid-4-verifiable-credential-issuance-1_0.html#section-8.2): "credential_identifier: REQUIRED when an Authorization Details of type openid_credential was returned from the Token Response. It MUST NOT be used otherwise." And: "credential_configuration_id: REQUIRED if a credential_identifiers parameter was not returned from the Token Response as part of the authorization_details parameter. It MUST NOT be used otherwise."
  - **The catalogue rules.** The configuration the grant authorized (the request's own, or the configuration of the object listing the requested identifier) is looked up in the catalogue; one it does not list is refused `unknown_credential_configuration` ([OID4VCI 1.0 Section 8.3.1.2](https://openid.net/specs/openid-4-verifiable-credential-issuance-1_0.html#section-8.3.1.2): "unknown_credential_configuration: Requested Credential Configuration is unknown."). A request by `credential_configuration_id` whose configuration declares a `scope` presents a token granted that scope, and a token with no `scope` claim grants none ([OID4VCI 1.0 Section 8.2](https://openid.net/specs/openid-4-verifiable-credential-issuance-1_0.html#section-8.2): "The corresponding object in the credential_configurations_supported map MUST contain one of the value(s) used in the scope parameter in the Authorization Request."); the scope binding holds a request by configuration only, a request by a granted identifier being authorized by the object that lists it; the key-attestation requirement judges the authorized configuration whichever member named it. Each refusal carries a fixed description that names no value from the request.
  - **The narrowed clause.** The clause of a configuration that declares no `scope` holds for a token that carries granted details, which names its configurations; a configuration that declares no `scope` is served to every token that carries no granted details, a token that carries a `scope` included, although [OID4VCI 1.0 Section 12.2.4](https://openid.net/specs/openid-4-verifiable-credential-issuance-1_0.html#section-12.2.4) says: "If scope is absent, the only way to request the Credential is using authorization_details [RFC9396] - in this case, the OAuth Authorization Server metadata for one of the Authorization Servers found from the Credential Issuer's Metadata must contain an authorization_details_types_supported that contains openid_credential." This is a limitation with its reason for each token: a Pre-Authorized Code token carries neither scope nor details, and its evidence, the Credential Offer, is seen by the application's seams alone, so the Credential Endpoint cannot tell such a token from one the clause refuses, and binding it to its offer's configurations is `IssueCredentialAsync`'s; a token that carries a `scope` and no granted details is served because the endpoint holds a request by configuration to the configuration's own declared `scope` (`Oid4VciEndpoints.ValidateScopeConfigurationBinding`) and has no rule for a configuration that declares none.
  - **The authorization-code path.** This is a limitation with its reason: the authorization-code client's token result surfaces no granted `authorization_details`, so on that path no library code learns the granted identifiers; the caller reads them from its own Token Response and passes `CredentialRequestTarget.ForIdentifier`.
  - **The wallet's target.** `CredentialRequestTarget` names what a Credential Request asks for, `ForConfiguration` or `ForIdentifier`, exactly one, so a request naming both members or neither cannot be expressed ([OID4VCI 1.0 Section 8.2](https://openid.net/specs/openid-4-verifiable-credential-issuance-1_0.html#section-8.2): "When this parameter is used, the credential_configuration_id MUST NOT be present."). `Oid4VciWalletClient.IssueWithAccessTokenDetailedAsync` takes it, and the request body writes exactly the member it names, escaped, as it writes the `credential_response_encryption` object's members. The Pre-Authorized Code entry points read their Token Response's `authorization_details` with the span reader and request by the first `credential_identifiers` value granted for the requested configuration, by the configuration when the response returned no `openid_credential` object, and send no request, answering `MalformedResponse`, when the response granted identifiers for other configurations only or any entry of the array is malformed (not an object, or an `openid_credential` object without its configuration, without identifiers, or listing an identifier that is empty or white space); the whole array is validated before a grant is answered ([OID4VCI 1.0 Section 6.2](https://openid.net/specs/openid-4-verifiable-credential-issuance-1_0.html#section-6.2): "The Wallet MUST use these identifiers together with an Access Token in subsequent Credential Requests."). An `authorization_details` member that is an empty array is malformed, and so is an entry whose `type` is absent or not a JSON string, wherever it stands; an entry whose `type` is a string other than `openid_credential` is skipped ([OID4VCI 1.0 Section 6.2](https://openid.net/specs/openid-4-verifiable-credential-issuance-1_0.html#section-6.2): "It is a non-empty array of objects, as defined in Section 7 of [RFC9396]." [RFC 9396 Section 2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9396#section-2): "type: An identifier for the authorization details type as a string. The value of the type field determines the allowable contents of the object that contains it. The value is unique for the described API in the context of the AS. This field is REQUIRED."). After an authorization-code exchange the caller reads the identifiers from its own Token Response and passes `ForIdentifier`.
  - Proven over the listener by `Oid4VciConfigurationConstraintsTests.ARequestByConfigurationUnderGrantedIdentifiersIsAnInvalidCredentialRequest`, `ARequestByAnotherConfigurationUnderGrantedIdentifiersIsAnInvalidCredentialRequest`, `AnIdentifierTheTokenWasNotGrantedIsUnknown`, `ARequestByIdentifierUnderATokenWithoutDetailsIsAnInvalidCredentialRequest`, `ATokenCarryingNoScopeIsRefusedAScopedConfiguration`, `AConfigurationTheCatalogueDoesNotListIsUnknown`, `AnIdentifierForAConfigurationRequiringKeyAttestationIsRefusedAPlainProof`, `AnIdentifierGrantedForAConfigurationTheCatalogueDoesNotListIsUnknown`, `AnIdentifierGrantedUnderTwoConfigurationsIsAServerError`, `AnIdentifierGrantedTwiceUnderOneConfigurationIsServed`, `ARequestByIdentifierIsNotHeldToTheConfigurationScope`, `ACredentialEndpointWithoutTheCatalogueIsNotServed`, `ACredentialRequestIsJudgedAgainstOneCatalogue` and `ACredentialRequestUnderACatalogueTheMetadataEndpointRefusesIsAServerError`; by `Oid4VciDeferredAndNotificationTests.ADeferredCredentialEndpointWithoutTheCatalogueIsNotServed`; by `Oid4VciEncryptionConformanceTests.ACredentialRequestAskingForResponseEncryptionTheCatalogueDoesNotAdvertiseIsRefused`, `ADeferredRequestAskingForResponseEncryptionTheCatalogueDoesNotAdvertiseIsRefused`, `ACredentialRequestAgainstAResponseEncryptionObjectWithoutBothListsIsRefused`, `ADeferredRequestAgainstAnEmptyResponseEncryptionObjectIsRefused`, `ARequiredResponseEncryptionWithoutItsListsIssuesNoCredential`, `ACredentialRequestUnderAnEncryptionObjectTheMetadataEndpointRefusesIsAServerError` and `ADeferredRequestUnderAStringEncryptionRequiredFlagIsAServerError`; by `Oid4VciAuthorizationCodeIssuanceTests.ParPkceAuthorizationCodeJourneyEndsInAnIssuedCredential`; and by `Oid4VciWalletClientTests.APreAuthorizedJourneyRequestsByTheGrantedIdentifier`, `APreAuthorizedTokenGrantingAnotherConfigurationIsNotSpentOnIt`, `AGrantedIdentifierThatNamesNoDatasetIsAMalformedTokenResponse`, `AConfigurationIdentifierIsWrittenAloneAndEscaped` and `AResponseEncryptionJwkMemberIsWrittenEscaped`. In process by `Oid4VciConfigurationConstraintsTests.ScopeMatchingConfigurationIssues` and `ScopeNotMatchingConfigurationIsRefused`; by `Oid4VciWalletClientTests.AMalformedEntryAfterAValidGrantIsAMalformedTokenResponse`, `AnEmptyAuthorizationDetailsArrayIsAMalformedTokenResponse`, `AnEntryWithoutAStringTypeIsAMalformedTokenResponse` and `AnEntryOfAnotherTypeBesideAValidGrantIsSkipped` (the library's Authorization Server emits no such member or entry, so the Token Response is delivered through the wallet's transport delegate); and by `CredentialRequestTargetTests`, the target's shape.

- **Key-attestation assurance constraints.** `KeyAttestationVerifier.CheckAssuranceConstraints` checks the attested `key_storage` and `user_authentication` arrays against the issuer's accepted-value sets as OID4VCI 1.0 Appendix D.1/D.2 define them: a MEMBERSHIP SET, satisfied when at least one attested value is a member of the accepted set by ordinal string equality, never an ordering between values; an absent or empty constraint constrains nothing, and an attested array that does not read as a JSON array of strings answers `KeyAttestationVerificationFailureReason.AssuranceConstraintValuesMalformed` rather than a silent unsatisfied result. Proven by `KeyAttestationVerifierTests.SatisfiedConstraintsValidate`, `KeyStorageConstraintUnsatisfiedWhenNoAttestedValueIsAccepted`, `UserAuthenticationConstraintUnsatisfiedWhenNoAttestedValueIsAccepted`, `MalformedAttestedArrayAnswersAssuranceConstraintValuesMalformed`, `AbsentConstraintIsSatisfiedByAnyAttestation`, and `EcosystemDefinedUrlValueMatchesByOrdinalEquality`.

- **Trace facts.** A request that matches no endpoint is categorized by a fixed precedence (`NoMatchCategories`): `NoRegistrationForTenant` when the tenant has no registration; otherwise `MatcherDeclined` when any candidate reached its matcher, else `CapabilityFiltered`, else `EndpointNameUnresolved` when `EndpointChain.UnresolvedUriCandidateNames` lists an endpoint name the application's URI resolver did not map, else `NoCandidateForPathAndMethod`. The unresolved names ride the `NoMatch` event under `ServerEventNames.NoMatchEndpointNameUnresolvedTagName`, and an unmapped name never stops a mapped endpoint from dispatching. `OAuthTagNames` tags the dispatch span from `IssueTokensAsync` — the one function every token-issuing grant calls — with `GrantType`, `ClientId`, `GrantedScope`, and the issued access token's `AccessTokenJti`; the code grant additionally tags `PkceMethod` from its own redeemed `codeState`, since PKCE is specific to that one grant. `ServerHttpResponse.ErrorCode` and `ServerTagNames.ErrorCode` carry the same wire error code onto the response and the span. No tag carries a token, a code, or a subject. Proven by `OAuthDiagnosticsTests.MappedEndpointStillDispatchesWhileAnotherNameIsUnresolved`, `CodeGrantTokenActivityCarriesTheOAuthWireFacts`, `ClientCredentialsTokenActivityCarriesItsOwnGrantType`, `RefusedTokenRequestActivityAndResponseCarryTheSameErrorCode`, and `NoActivityTagCarriesTheAccessTokenAuthorizationCodeOrSubject`.

- **Family revocation and persistence.** Valid reuse first reads its predecessor's audit, then revokes its own audit and walks `SuccessorRefreshFlowId` through retained records. Each audited token reaches the optional `RevokeIssuedTokenAsync`; the live refresh record is claimed with its loaded step before the required `DeleteFlowStateAsync` deletes it. A lost claim reloads that same flow once and follows the retired successor. A second failed live claim stops without deleting unclaimed state. Missing or unexpected records stop traversal. There is no arbitrary hop cap: retention by `ExpiresAt` supplies the natural bound, and a visited set stops corruption-induced cycles while keeping completed revocations. After the walk, `ServerRefreshTokenReuseDetected` sets `RevokedAt` through a pure record-copy transition and the runner saves it. Sequential presentations skip the walk; concurrent reuses may repeat idempotent audit calls. Code replay uses the same successor walk. Every reuse refusal, including issuer-resolution failure, has the constant `invalid_grant` body; only response bytes are constant, not work or timing. A legitimate client's concurrent refresh can lose its grant when one request loads the retired record after another rotates it: OAuth 2.1 §4.3.1 states "The authorization server cannot determine which party submitted the invalid refresh token, but it will revoke the active refresh token as well as the access authorization grant associated with it."

- **PKCE policy.** Plain is refused everywhere, including `PolicyProfile.Rfc6749WithPkce`. [OAuth 2.1 draft-16 §4.1.1](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-ietf-oauth-v2-1-16#section-4.1.1) prohibits the plain transformation; [§7.5.2](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-ietf-oauth-v2-1-16#section-7.5.2) states "The plain code challenge method, defined in [RFC7636], is explicitly forbidden in OAuth 2.1." [RFC 9700 §2.1.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9700#section-2.1.1) calls for a method that does not expose the verifier; S256 is the only such method. PAR, direct authorize, and both JAR entry points refuse unsupported methods with `invalid_request` per [RFC 7636 §4.4.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7636#section-4.4.1). The RFC 6749 profile retains its other relaxations. [RFC 8414 §2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8414#section-2) discovery advertises `["S256"]` under every profile. `WellKnownCodeChallengeMethods.Plain` and `IsPlain` identify the refused wire name and values in remote client metadata; they do not enable server acceptance. The persisted `ServerCodeIssuedState.CodeChallengeMethod` travels from authorization to token verification: only S256 hashes and compares, and every other persisted value fails `invalid_grant`. [RFC 7636 §4.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7636#section-4.1) verifier grammar is checked before [§4.6](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7636#section-4.6) digest verification. Absent methods request plain under [RFC 7636 §4.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7636#section-4.3) and receive the same refusal. Both JAR paths check the method immediately after signature verification, before projection and loopback redirect matching; parameter failures use the direct JSON response specified by [RFC 9101 §6.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9101#section-6.3). Direct authorization validates the client and redirect URI first, then redirects `invalid_request` with the exact request state under [RFC 6749 §4.1.2.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6749#section-4.1.2.1). Invalid destinations receive a direct refusal. PAR retains its JSON 400 under [RFC 9126 §2.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9126#section-2.3).
  - `AbsentPkceMethodRefusedAtParUnderRfc6749WithPkce` and `AbsentPkceMethodRefusedAtDirectAuthorizeUnderRfc6749WithPkce` prove the absent-method refusal.
  - `JarAuthorizeByValueTests` and `JarParTests` each prove plain and absent methods with an exact registered redirect and with a native loopback redirect on an ephemeral port.
  - `LoopbackFallbackRefusesPlainPkceIndependently` directly proves the fallback's independent S256 condition; `PlainPkceWithInvalidRedirectUriDoesNotRedirect` proves destination validation precedes the redirect error.
  - `AuthCodeParPkceRealWireFlowTests.PlainPkceRefusedAtParUnderS256OnlyDefault`, `PlainPkceRefusedAtParUnderRfc6749WithPkce`, and `PlainPkceRefusedAtDirectAuthorizeUnderRfc6749WithPkce` prove the authorization refusals over the listener.
  - `JarAuthorizeByValueTests.RejectsJarWithCodeChallengeMethodPlain` and `JarParTests.RejectsJarWithCodeChallengeMethodPlain` prove signed-request refusals under the RFC 6749 profile over the listener.
  - `DiscoveryEndpointTests.DiscoveryEmitsCodeChallengeMethodsAsS256OnlyUnderFapi20` and `DiscoveryEmitsCodeChallengeMethodsAsS256OnlyUnderRfc6749WithPkce` prove the listener advertisement.
  - `AuthCodeParPkceRealWireFlowTests.TooShortCodeVerifierAtTokenExchangeIsInvalidGrant` and `CodeVerifierWithDisallowedCharacterAtTokenExchangeIsInvalidGrant` use matching S256 digests to isolate the grammar rules.
  - `PersistedPlainPkceMethodAtTokenExchangeIsInvalidGrant` and `PersistedUnknownPkceMethodAtTokenExchangeIsInvalidGrant` corrupt the host's stored method and prove refusal; `S256IssuedCodeVerifiedAgainstPersistedMethodNotRequest` proves the persisted S256 transformation, and `FullJourneyReachesTokenRefreshAndRevocation` proves successful S256 issuance.

- **CAEP `credential-change` emit + credential status lists (the credential-side signal).** The signal/command spectrum extends from the *session* to the *credential*: where `session-revoked` (Slice 7) signals a revoked session, a CAEP `credential-change` SET (CAEP 1.0 §3.3, `change_type: revoke`, `credential_type: verifiable-credential`) signals a revoked credential — the same best-effort "signal" end, the same `SecurityEventTokenIssuance` / reception subsystem, the same **no library orchestrator** rule. The status runtime itself lives *outside* the AS, in `Verifiable.Core/StatusLists`: one shared bit core (`StatusList` — packed N-bit entries over a pooled buffer, parameterized by a **required** `BitOrder`: least-significant-first for the IETF Token Status List, most-significant-first for the W3C Bitstring Status List) carrying two presentations — `StatusListToken` (IETF: ZLIB + base64url token) and `BitstringStatusListCodec` / `BitstringStatusListEntry` / `BitstringStatusListValidation` (W3C: GZIP + Multibase + the `BitstringStatusListCredential`, issued/verified over the existing JWS or Data Integrity surface). Revocation is the `UpdateCredentialStatusesDelegate` seam — the credential-side counterpart of `RevokeSubjectTokensDelegate`: the application flips the bit(s) and republishes each affected list once (the pull channel a verifier polls) and emits the `credential-change` SET to its Shared Signals receivers (the push channel). The seam is **batch-shaped** because a single credential may carry several entries across one or more lists (W3C §A.3/§A.4), grouped so a batch re-encodes each affected list exactly once with no half-applied intermediate. Proven by `BitstringStatusListRevocationDualChannelHttpTests` (one trigger → republished list + pushed SET, each verified firewalled) and `BitstringStatusListBatchRevocationTests`. Wire shapes verified against the verbatim specifications: Token Status List `draft-ietf-oauth-status-list-20` §4.1, CAEP 1.0 §3.3, RFC 8417 (SET), RFC 9493 (`sub_id`), and RFC 8935 (push delivery).

- **The federation resolve endpoint names its error.** `AuthorizationServerIntegration.ResolveSubjectTrustChainAsync` (`ResolveSubjectTrustChainDelegate`) answers a `FederationResolveOutcome` — `FederationResolveOutcome.Resolved(contribution)` or `FederationResolveOutcome.Failed(error)` naming a `FederationResolveError`, or `null` — which `FederationEndpoints.BuildFederationResolveErrorResponse` maps to the [Federation §8.9](https://openid.net/specs/openid-federation-1_0.html#section-8.9) error vocabulary the specification assigns each reason (`invalid_trust_anchor` 404, `invalid_trust_chain` 400, `invalid_metadata` 400, `not_found` 404, and `invalid_subject` 404 for `null` or an unnamed error). `FederationEffectiveMetadataResolver.ReadDeclaredEntityTypes` enumerates every Entity Type the resolved subject declares, in ordinal order, for the request's optional `entity_type` filter. Proven by `FederationResolveEndpointTests` (`ResolveEndpointServesSignedResolveResponse`, `ResolveEndpointReturns404WhenSubjectUnresolvable`, `ResolveEndpointReturns400WithInvalidTrustChainWhenResolverNamesIt`, `ResolveEndpointReturns400WithInvalidMetadataWhenResolverNamesIt`, `ResolveEndpointReturns404WithInvalidTrustAnchorWhenResolverNamesIt`, `ResolveEndpointReturns404WithNotFoundWhenResolverNamesIt`) and `FederationEffectiveMetadataResolverTests.ReadDeclaredEntityTypesReturnsEveryDeclaredTypeInOrdinalOrder` / `ReadDeclaredEntityTypesReturnsEmptyWhenSubjectDeclaresNone`.

- **Remote documents: the library performs the attempt, the application owns the cache.** Caching is an application-layer concern. `ClientIdMetadataDocuments.ResolveAsync` and `JwksUriResolver.ResolveAsync` are stateless: the policed fetch through `OutboundFetch.FetchAsync`, the document's validation, and the RFC 9111 §5.2 freshness the headers imply, reported on the result as `HttpCacheFreshness`. The application's implementations of `ResolveClientMetadataDelegate` and `ResolveJwksUriDelegate` are the cache — the store, the lifetime bounds, what follows a failure, and RFC 9111 §4.2.4's rule against serving stale without permission are theirs — and one key-set delegate instance is wired into both `ClientIdMetadataDocumentResolverOptions.ResolveJwksUri` and `PrivateKeyJwtClientAuthentication.BuildValidator`, so a document and a registration share one cache. A cached document whose `HasJwksUriKeySet` is set is refreshed on a hit through `ClientIdMetadataDocuments.RefreshJwksAsync`, so a rotated key set follows its own lifetime and not the document's. The library ships no in-process dictionary, no lifetime options and no retry floor; `ClientMetadataResolutionCache` in `test/Verifiable.Tests/TestInfrastructure` is the reference an application starts from. See [§4.2](#42-remote-documents-the-attempt-is-the-librarys-the-cache-is-the-applications) and `documents/ADRs/remote-documents-attempt-and-application-cache.md`.

- **The SD-JWT VC issuer key is resolved through one delegate.** `ResolveIssuerKeyDelegate` (`issuerId`, `keyId`, `x5c`, `ExchangeContext`) is the application's single seam for both of [SD-JWT VC draft-19 §2.5](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-ietf-oauth-sd-jwt-vc-19#section-2.5)'s key-discovery mechanisms — JWT VC Issuer Metadata (`JwtVcIssuerMetadataDocuments.ResolveAsync` / `SelectKeyAsync`, `JwtVcIssuerMetadataResolution` and its outcome enum, `JwtVcIssuerMetadataDocumentResolverOptions`, `WellKnownPaths.JwtVcIssuer`) or Inline X.509 Certificates (`SdJwtX5cTrustResolver.ResolveAsync`, evaluated per call against a caller-supplied instant, never a captured `TimeProvider`) — or an application's own trust framework entirely; `SdJwtVpTokenVerification.VerifyAsync` requires the calling context so the delegate can police its own outbound request. The CBOR mdoc sibling, `MdocCborIacaTrustResolver.Create`, takes a `TimeProvider` instead because its returned delegate is built once and reused across many resolutions. Proven by `SdJwtVpTokenVerificationIssuerKeySeamTests` (`VerifyAsyncPassesTheHeaderKidToTheDelegateAndNullX5cWhenAbsent`, `VerifyAsyncPassesTheHeaderX5cToTheDelegate`, `VerifyAsyncVerifiesCredentialSignatureThroughX5cTrustResolverWithConfiguredAnchor`, `VerifyAsyncReportsCredentialSignatureUnverifiedWithNoMatchingAnchor`, `VerifyAsyncPassesTheCallersContextToTheDelegate`) and `JwtVcIssuerMetadataDocumentsResolvingTests` (`SelectKeyAsyncSelectsByKeyIdFromInlineJwks`, `SelectKeyAsyncSelectsThroughJwksUriResolver`, `SelectKeyAsyncRefusesADuplicateKeyId`, `SelectKeyAsyncSelectsTheSoleKeyWhenNoKeyIdIsPresented`, `SelectKeyAsyncRefusesWhenNoKeyIdIsPresentedAndTwoKeysExist`); the real-wire round trip is `Oid4VpFlowIntegrationTests.IssuerKeyResolvesFromJwtVcIssuerMetadataOverRealWireThenViaJwksUri`.

- **Every client-side dial is evaluated against the outbound policy first.** The endpoints the client flows dial — PAR, token, refresh, revocation, the ID-JAG mint and redeem, the OID4VCI wallet's metadata, nonce, credential, deferred and notification calls, the OID4VP wallet's response and request-object posts — are named by discovered metadata or by an offer, and `OutboundRequest`'s remarks say a POST to such an endpoint is as much an SSRF vector as a fetched URL. Each flow evaluates `OutboundFetchPolicy` before invoking the application's transport, from the call's `ExchangeContext` when it carries one and otherwise from the client's deployment configuration (`OAuthClientInfrastructure.OutboundFetchPolicy`, `Oid4VciWalletConfiguration.OutboundFetchPolicy`, `Oid4VpWalletConfiguration.OutboundFetchPolicy`, default `SecureDefault`), and a denied target answers with the flow's own typed failure without the transport ever being called. The three OID4VCI transport delegates carry the `ExchangeContext` for the same reason `SendFormPostDelegate` does. The browser cookie concern is the application's transport factory's to close (`credentials: omit` on every request built for these seams); the seam docs say so. The OID4VCI wallet's OPTIONAL §4.1.3 by-reference Credential Offer GET (`Oid4VciWalletConfiguration.FetchCredentialOffer`) is the one client dial that does not follow this evaluate-then-invoke shape: it runs through the library's own `OutboundFetch.FetchAsync` chokepoint — the same one server-side document resolution uses — rather than a single-hop transport delegate, so it carries that chokepoint's own redirect re-validation and per-hop policy evaluation, bounded by `MaximumCredentialOfferBytes`. See [§4.2](#42-remote-documents-the-attempt-is-the-librarys-the-cache-is-the-applications).

- **The client starts an authorization request directly, and asks for a token as itself.** `AuthCodeClient.StartAsync` composes the [RFC 6749 §4.1.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6749#section-4.1.1) authorization redirect with PKCE `S256` ([RFC 7636 §4.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7636#section-4.3)) and persists the same flow state the pushed road (`StartParAsync`) persists, so the callback and token legs serve both roads. It is refused before any state, PKCE material or redirect is built when the authorization server's metadata sets `require_pushed_authorization_requests` ([RFC 9126 §5](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9126#section-5)), and a caller field named after a flow-owned parameter never overrides the flow's value. `ClientCredentialsClient.RequestTokenAsync`, behind the `ClientCredentials` extension property on `OAuthClient`, composes the [RFC 6749 §4.4.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6749#section-4.4.2) request, authenticates by `ClientRegistration.AuthenticationMethod`, and sends through the DPoP-retrying token request the authorization-code and refresh legs use (`TokenEndpointClientOperations`), so a wired infrastructure presents the proof and answers the one `use_dpop_nonce` challenge. The ID-JAG mint and redeem roads (`IdJagFlowHandlers.MintAsync`, `RedeemAsync`) send through the same request while keeping their own `OAuthOutboundFetchPolicyDenied` answer. Client authentication is attached per attempt, so a `private_key_jwt` retry presents a fresh assertion (a new `jti` and `iat`) and an authorization server that enforces client-assertion replay (`CheckClientAssertionJtiReplayDelegate`) serves it (`RequestCompletesAfterOneNonceRetryWithFreshClientAssertionUnderReplayCheckedRegistration`, `RedeemCompletesAfterOneNonceRetryWithFreshClientAssertionUnderReplayCheckedRegistration`, `MintCompletesAfterOneNonceRetryOverRealWireUnderMandatingRegistration`). Proven by `AuthCodeParPkceRealWireFlowTests.PlainStartReachesTokenOverRealWire`, `StartAsyncRefusedBeforeAnyDialWhenMetadataRequiresPar`, `AdditionalFieldsCannotOverrideStateOrCodeChallengeAtPlainStart`, and `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.ClientComposedRequestIssuesTokenUnderClientSecretBasicOverRealWire`, `ClientComposedRequestIssuesTokenUnderPrivateKeyJwtOverRealWire`, `WrongSecretAnswersTypedInvalidClientFailureNeverAnException`, `TokenEndpointDeniedByOutboundPolicyIsNeverDialed`, and `ClientRequestCompletesAfterOneNonceRetryOverRealWire`.

- **The OID4VCI wallet's transports carry headers, and its requests carry DPoP.** `Oid4VciFormPostDelegate` and `Oid4VciJsonPostDelegate` take an `OutgoingHeaders` parameter and answer an `HttpResponseData`, so the client — not the transport — composes the `DPoP` proof header; `Oid4VciWalletConfiguration.ConstructDpopProofAsync`, `DpopKey`, and `GenerateIdentifierAsync` are wired together or not at all (`Oid4VciWalletClient`'s constructor refuses a partial set), and when wired the §6 Pre-Authorized Code Token Request carries a proof and every subsequent §7/§8/§9/§11 request does too once the Token Response returns `token_type=DPoP`. `LookupDpopNonce` / `StoreDpopNonce` cache the server-issued nonce per authority; `DpopNonceRetry.SendWithNonceRetryAsync` sends once and retries exactly one further time with a fresh proof on a `use_dpop_nonce` challenge ([RFC 9449 §8](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9449#section-8), §9), never a second time. `CredentialIssuanceResult.ExpiresAt` is the Token Request instant plus `expires_in`, or `null` when the response carried none. Proven by `ConstructWithDpopProofConstructorButNoDpopKeyThrows`, `ConstructWithDpopKeyButNoProofConstructorThrows`, `ConstructWithDpopKeyAndProofConstructorButNoGeneratorThrows`, `TokenRequestCarriesDpopHeaderWhenKeyConfiguredAndNoneWhenAbsent`, `TokenRequestRetriesOnceOnUseDpopNonceChallengeAndUsesTheSecondAnswer`, `TokenRequestFailsOnSecondUseDpopNonceChallengeRatherThanRetryingAgain`, `CredentialRequestRetriesOnceOnUseDpopNonceChallengeAndUsesTheSecondAnswer`, `CredentialRequestFailsOnSecondUseDpopNonceChallengeRatherThanRetryingAgain`, and `ExpiresAtEqualsRequestedAtPlusExpiresInWhenPresentAndIsNullWhenAbsent`.

- **The OID4VCI wallet answers remote failures as values.** `Oid4VciRequestFailure` (`Kind`, `Endpoint`, nullable `StatusCode`, `ErrorCode`, `ErrorDescription`) and `Oid4VciRequestFailureKind` (`ErrorResponse`, `MalformedResponse`, `OutboundPolicyDenied`) carry every `Oid4VciWalletClient` request's failure on input the Wallet does not control; every such request answers `Result<T, Oid4VciRequestFailure>` and `SendCredentialNotificationAsync` answers a nullable `Oid4VciRequestFailure?` — `null` on the §11.2 2xx acknowledgment. What the remote party sent never throws (`CredentialOfferSerializer.TryParse` is the non-throwing parse `FromJson` shares); composition errors, caller errors, and an application delegate's own exception stay exceptions. Proven by `TokenRequestRefusedAnswersErrorResponseWithStatusAndCode`, `TokenResponseRepeatingAccessTokenAnswersMalformedResponse`, `NonceRequestRefusedAnswersErrorResponseWithStatusAndCode`, `NonceResponseWithoutCNonceAnswersMalformedResponse`, `CredentialRequestRefusedAnswersErrorResponseWithStatusAndCode`, `CredentialResponseWithEmptyCredentialsArrayAnswersMalformedResponse`, and `NotificationRefusedAnswersErrorResponseWithStatusAndCode`.

- **Capability catalogs enumerate themselves.** `WellKnownCapabilityIdentifiers.All`, `WellKnownFederationCapabilityIdentifiers.All`, `WellKnownVcalmCapabilities.All`, and `WellKnownWebFingerCapabilityIdentifiers.All` are hand-kept lists, one per catalog file, that name every `CapabilityIdentifier` property the same file declares — an operator-typed capability URN is then checked against a complete set rather than one that drifts. `SourceHygieneTests.CapabilityIdentifierCatalogsEnumerateEveryDeclaredIdentifier` scans all four files and fails naming the file and line of any property a catalog's list omits, or any list entry no property declares.

- **The tenant key never leaves the process; the tenant handle is what the library shows.** `TenantId` scopes storage, correlation and the application's own delegates, renders nothing of its value, and reaches no span, body, claim or document. `TenantHandle`, set by the application on `ClientRecord`, is the only tenant-shaped value the library emits: the dispatch span's `server.tenant.handle` tag, fail closed when no handle is set, and the display half of the registration projection, whose key half stays for observer correlation. The library derives no handle. See [§4.3](#43-the-tenant-key-and-the-tenant-handle).

- **The client assertion names the issuer as its audience, its type is a per-registration choice, and the server's audience rule is a policy.** [rfc7523bis-11 §4](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-ietf-oauth-rfc7523bis-11#section-4) item b replaces RFC 7523 §3 item 3 for client authentication: "the aud (audience) claim value MUST use the issuer identifier [RFC8414] of the authorization server as its sole value. The authorization server MUST have an issuer identifier to be used with this specification. The aud claim value MUST indicate only the issuer identifier of the intended authorization server as the audience of the JWT; this includes that the token endpoint URL of the authorization server MUST NOT be used as an audience value. The authorization server MUST reject any JWT that does not contain its issuer identifier as its sole audience value." "In the absence of an application profile specifying otherwise, applications MUST compare the audience values using the Simple String Comparison method defined in Section 6.2.1 of RFC 3986 [RFC3986]." [§5](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-ietf-oauth-rfc7523bis-11#section-5) replaces the last paragraph of [RFC 9126 §2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9126#section-2) for a pushed authorization request: "the issuer identifier URL of the authorization server according to [RFC8414] MUST be used as the sole value of the audience. The authorization server MUST reject any such JWT that does not contain its own issuer identifier as the sole audience value." Both sections add: "Client authentication JWTs SHOULD be explicitly typed by using the typ header parameter value client-authentication+jwt or another more specific explicit type value defined by a specification profiling this specification", and "it is NOT RECOMMENDED for servers to reject JWTs that do not have explicit types." [RFC 9126 §2.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9126#section-2.1) has the authorization server "Authenticate the client in the same way as at the token endpoint (Section 2.3 of [RFC6749])." Of the client authentication parameters a pushed request carries, the same section says "they are relied upon only for client authentication and are not germane to the authorization request itself", so the pushed request the server stores holds neither the `client_assertion` nor the `client_assertion_type` value. [OpenID Connect Core §9](https://openid.net/specs/openid-connect-core-1_0.html#ClientAuthentication) says of the `jti`: "These tokens MUST only be used once, unless conditions for reuse were negotiated between the parties; any such negotiation is beyond the scope of this specification." and of the claim set: "Any Claims used that are not understood MUST be ignored." On the client, `ClientRegistration.ClientAssertionAudience` names the `aud` value at every endpoint the registration authenticates at: `ClientAssertionAudience.IssuerIdentifier` by default, `ClientAssertionAudience.TokenEndpoint` on offer. `ClientRegistration.IsClientAssertionExplicitlyTyped` (default `true`) selects the `typ` header parameter, `JWT` or `client-authentication+jwt`, through the `type` parameter of `ClientAssertionSigning.SignAsync`, and `ClientTokenEndpointAuthentication.AttachClientAssertionAsync` applies both choices to the assertion it attaches, so the code exchange, the refresh, the client credentials grant and a raw push present one shape. On the server, `PrivateKeyJwtClientAuthentication.BuildValidator` takes a `ClientAssertionAudiencePolicy`: the default `AuthorizationServerIdentities` accepts the issuer identifier and the request's token and pushed-authorization endpoint URLs, and `IssuerIdentifierOnly` is the opt-in that accepts the issuer identifier as the sole `aud` value, compared ordinally; combining `additionalAcceptedAudiences` with `IssuerIdentifierOnly` throws an `ArgumentException` when the validator is built. The validator reads no `typ`, so both `JWT` and `client-authentication+jwt` authenticate, reads the named claims only and ignores any other, and fails closed when the issuer identifier cannot be resolved. The application's turn: the registration's audience choice; the server's policy choice; the issuer identifier and the endpoint URLs the server accepts as the audience, which the application pins to configuration because a value derived from an unvalidated `Host` or forwarded header lets an assertion made for another authorization server match; `checkJtiReplayAsync` as the single-use mechanism, since a validator built without it leaves the single-use MUST to the application's own mechanism; and a `client_secret_jwt` validator, which the library does not ship, so the application applies the audience rule there itself. The library's own pushed-request sender attaches no client authentication; a confidential client's pushed request is authenticated by the application's push built on `ClientTokenEndpointAuthentication.AttachClientAssertionAsync`. Three deviations are named: the default audience policy, and two per-registration opt-ins. `ClientAssertionAudience.TokenEndpoint` is a deviation from §4 item b's MUST NOT, offered only for an authorization server that refuses its issuer identifier as the audience. The default `ClientAssertionAudiencePolicy.AuthorizationServerIdentities` is a deviation from §4 item b and §5's MUST reject a JWT without the issuer identifier as its sole audience value, taken because the text is an Internet-Draft and because of the asymmetry its §6 describes: "the resulting compatibility is not entirely symmetric", so a server enforcing the narrower rule refuses a client that follows RFC 7523 §3 item 3 or OpenID Connect Core §9. The default of `IsClientAssertionExplicitlyTyped` follows the §4 and §5 SHOULD, typing the assertion `client-authentication+jwt`; setting it to `false` writes the generic `JWT`, a deviation from that SHOULD, offered only for an authorization server that rejects `typ` values it does not know. Proven by `AuthCodeClientAuthenticationTests.CodeExchangeAssertionFollowsTheRegistrationsAudienceAndTypeAsync`, `AuthCodeClientAuthenticationTests.IssuerOnlyPolicyAcceptsOnlyTheIssuerAsSoleAudienceAsync`, `AuthCodeClientAuthenticationTests.PushedRequestAcceptsEachOfTheThreeRfc9126AudiencesWithoutExplicitWiring`, `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.ClientCredentialsAssertionNamesTheIssuerByDefaultAsync`, `ClientCredentialsGrantTests.IssuerOnlyPolicyHoldsAtTheTokenEndpointAsync`, `ClientAssertionSigningTests.TypeHeaderIsTheValueTheCallerNames`, `PrivateKeyJwtClientAuthenticationTests.IssuerOnlyPolicyWithAdditionalAudiencesIsRefusedAtBuild`, `PrivateKeyJwtClientAuthenticationTests.AssertionIsRefusedWhenIssuerResolutionFails`, `PrivateKeyJwtClientAuthenticationTests.IssuerOnlyPolicyCountsAnAudienceSequenceHeldAsAnIterator`, `ClientAssertionSigningTests.UnsetAssertionAudienceIsRefusedAsAnArgumentAndAddsNothingToTheForm`, `AuthCodeClientAuthenticationTests.ClaimSetsTheSpecificationsAllowAreAcceptedAtThePushedRequestAsync`, `AuthCodeClientAuthenticationTests.ClaimRuleViolationsAreRefusedAtThePushedRequestAsync`, `AuthCodeClientAuthenticationTests.FutureNotBeforeIsRefusedAtThePushedRequestAsync`, `AuthCodeClientAuthenticationTests.PushedRequestStoresNoClientAuthenticationParametersAsync`, and `RefreshConfidentialClientAuthenticationTests.RefreshAssertionNamesTheIssuerByDefaultAsync`.

- **Resource indicators: the grammar, the fold and the comparison key.** [RFC 8707 §2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8707#section-2) says a `resource` value "MUST be an absolute URI, as specified by Section 4.3 of [RFC3986]. The URI MUST NOT include a fragment component.", provides the error code `invalid_target` for both request kinds ("The following error code is provided for an authorization server to indicate problems with the requested resource(s) in response to an authorization request or access token request.") and defines it ("The requested resource is invalid, missing, unknown, or malformed."). [§2.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8707#section-2.1) is the authorization request's section and directs: "If the authorization server fails to parse the provided value(s) or does not consider the resource(s) acceptable, it should reject the request with an error response using the error code "invalid_target" as the value of the "error" parameter and can provide additional information regarding the reasons for the error using the "error_description"." This library applies the same reading at the token endpoint as its own reading. `ResourceIndicatorSyntax.Check` reads a value's original spelling against [RFC 3986 Appendix A](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc3986#appendix-A)'s `absolute-URI` and names the first part that fails as a `ResourceIndicatorFault`: every code point is US-ASCII ([RFC 3986 §2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc3986#section-2): "The ABNF notation defines its terminal values to be non-negative integers (codepoints) based on the US-ASCII coded character set"), every `%` begins a two-digit triplet, a `#` is refused, and a value without a scheme is refused ([RFC 3986 §3.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc3986#section-3.1): "Scheme names consist of a sequence of characters beginning with a letter and followed by any combination of letters, digits, plus ("+"), period ("."), or hyphen ("-")."), so `:foo` and `://api.example.com/`, whose scheme is empty, are refused. For an `http` or `https` value `Check` adds [RFC 9110 §4.2.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110#section-4.2.1) and [§4.2.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110#section-4.2.2)'s "A recipient that processes such a URI reference MUST reject it as invalid." for an empty host (`HttpHost`) and the `"://" authority` production for a value without one (`HttpAuthority`), and [RFC 9110 §4.2.4](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110#section-4.2.4)'s "a recipient SHOULD parse for userinfo and treat its presence as an error; it is likely being used to obscure the authority for the sake of phishing attacks." for a `userinfo` (`HttpUserInfo`), so `https://api.example.com@other.example/` is refused instead of reading as one host while naming another. For every other scheme the generic syntax's `userinfo` is accepted, stored and minted into `aud` as sent, the deprecated `user:password` form included; the deviation is named below. [RFC 6749 §3.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6749#section-3.1) is followed as written: "Parameters sent without a value MUST be treated as if they were omitted from the request." `ReadResourceOccurrences` drops an empty occurrence at every form ingress, the token exchange's included, so a request whose occurrences are all empty names no resource, while a whitespace-only occurrence is a value with no indicator in it and is malformed; a blank string inside a request object's `resource` claim is not a parameter sent without a value and is refused as malformed by the claim reader.
  - **The fold and the comparison key.** [RFC 8707 §2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8707#section-2): "The authorization server may use the exact "resource" value as the audience or it may map from that value to a more general URI or abstract identifier for the given resource." The library maps by folding: `ResourceIndicatorSyntax.Fold` lowers the ASCII letters of an accepted value's scheme and, when it has an authority, of its host outside percent triplets, following [RFC 3986 §6.2.2.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc3986#section-6.2.2.1) ("the scheme and host are case-insensitive and therefore should be normalized to lowercase") and the [MCP authorization](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2026-07-28/basic/authorization#canonical-server-uri) sentence "implementations SHOULD accept uppercase scheme and host components for robustness and interoperability." The fold is culture-independent ASCII lowering; userinfo, port, path and query keep their spelling ("The other generic syntax components are assumed to be case-sensitive unless specifically defined otherwise by the scheme"). A value `Check` refuses comes back from `Fold` unchanged. `ResourceIndicatorSyntax.ComparisonKey` is the fold of an accepted value and the value itself otherwise, which is [RFC 3986 §6.2.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc3986#section-6.2.1)'s simple string comparison: "If two URIs, when considered as character strings, are identical, then it is safe to conclude that they are equivalent." `ResolveEffectiveResource` matches a token request's `resource` values against the granted set by `ComparisonKey` ([RFC 8707 §2.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8707#section-2.2): "the authorization server will issue an access token based on that subset of requested resources, whereas any refresh token that is returned is bound to the full original grant."): a requested value matches the granted entry of its identical spelling first and otherwise the first granted entry with its comparison key, and the issued `aud` carries each matched granted entry once, in the requested order and in the granted spelling. The granted set and the requested set are each deduplicated by exact spelling. Because the path is compared as sent, a multi-tenant resource that names its tenant in a path component keeps it ([RFC 8707 §3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8707#section-3): "it is important to use a specific resource URI including any portion of the URI that identifies the tenant, such as a path component.").
  - **The gates.** A `resource` value enters from the wire at four sites and each folds at its own site: `ReadResource` for the form field (the pushed request, the direct authorize request, and the token endpoint of the authorization-code, refresh and `client_credentials` grants), `AuthCodeRequestObjectExtensions.ReadResourceClaim` for the request object's claim (a JSON string or an array of strings, [RFC 8707 §2.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8707#section-2.1): "a single "resource" parameter value is represented as a JSON string while multiple values are represented as an array of strings"), the token exchange grant at its own site, which reads `ReadResourceOccurrences`, and the `resource` claim of a signed ID-JAG at the redeem, which `IdJagAssertionValidation`'s claim core reads. That claim is absent, a JSON string, or a non-empty JSON array of JSON strings, each value checked by `ResourceIndicatorSyntax.IsAbsoluteResourceIndicator` and surfaced folded in `IdJagAssertionValidationResult.Resource` in the order given ([draft-ietf-oauth-identity-assertion-authz-grant-04 §4.4.1](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-ietf-oauth-identity-assertion-authz-grant-04#section-4.4.1): "If the resource claim is present, the Resource Authorization Server MUST process it according to Section 2 of [RFC8707]."); an empty string, an empty array, any other JSON type (a number, a boolean, an object, a JSON `null`), an element that is not a JSON string (a `null` element included) and a value the grammar refuses fail the validation as `IdJagValidationFailureReason.MalformedResource` before the `sub_id` step, and the application's `ValidateJwtBearerAssertionDelegate` is to return no grant for a failed result, so the token endpoint answers `invalid_grant` ([RFC 7523 §3.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7523#section-3.1): "If the JWT is not valid, or the current time is not within the token's valid time window for use, the authorization server constructs an error response as defined in OAuth 2.0 [RFC6749]. The value of the "error" parameter MUST be the "invalid_grant" error code."). Answering `invalid_grant` there, and not `invalid_target`, is this library's reading, named with its reason in the deviations below; so is refusing the empty string and the empty array. The fold reaches the `resource` claim only: the grant's own `aud` is compared as signed, ordinally, so an `aud` that differs from the server's issuer only in the case of its scheme or host is `IdJagValidationFailureReason.AudienceMismatch` ([RFC 7523 §3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7523#section-3) item 3: "In the absence of an application profile specifying otherwise, compliant applications MUST compare the audience values using the Simple String Comparison method defined in Section 6.2.1 of RFC 3986 [RFC3986]."). At the form, request-object and token-exchange sites, `ValidateResourceOccurrences` checks every occurrence as received at the five form ingresses (the pushed request, the direct authorization request, the code and refresh redemptions in each step endpoint's pre-correlation step, so the answer is byte-identical for a grant that does not exist and one that does, and the `client_credentials` grant); `ValidateResourceIndicatorsShape` checks the request object's claim at the pushed and the by-value request object; the token exchange checks its own occurrences in its own request parsing. The refusal is `invalid_target` with one fixed `error_description`, "The resource parameter must be an absolute URI (RFC 3986 section 4.3) without a fragment.", never the value, and the request's trace carries the event `oauth.resource.malformed` with the tag `oauth.resource.fault`, never the value: at every form ingress the tag names the first failing part of the value, and a request object's `resource` claim that is not a JSON string or an array of strings, or holds a blank, non-string or whitespace-bearing element, is recorded as `Fragment`, the fault of the placeholder the claim reader hands the gate in its place (any other refused element is recorded by its own first failing part). A token request naming a resource outside the granted set is refused `invalid_target` with one fixed `error_description`, "The resource parameter must be a subset of the resource(s) originally granted." (or "The resource parameter must be a subset of the resource(s) originally granted; none were granted." when the grant carries none), and the refresh grant's `invalid_scope` refusal of a scope token that was not granted answers one fixed `error_description` the same way, "The scope parameter must be a subset of the scope originally granted."; neither names a requested value. The token exchange's `audience` is read occurrence by occurrence and never folded: an empty occurrence is dropped ([RFC 6749 §3.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6749#section-3.1)) and a whitespace-only one is refused `invalid_request` ([RFC 8693 §2.2.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8693#section-2.2.2): "The value of the "error" parameter MUST be the "invalid_request" error code."). The description sits inside [RFC 6749 §5.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6749#section-5.2)'s set ("Values for the "error_description" parameter MUST NOT include characters outside the set %x20-21 / %x23-5B / %x5D-7E."), and the authorize error redirect, in both its plain and its JARM branch, reduces every description it carries, an application's denial description included, to that set ([§4.1.2.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6749#section-4.1.2.1) states the same rule).
  - **The carry of an exchange's audience.** A refresh token the token exchange issues stores the `audience` values the authorization seam authorized as its granted set, in the slot a resource indicator is stored in, under the carry rule of `IsCarriedAudienceEntry` and not the grammar of an incoming `resource`: every entry holds no white space and parses as an absolute URI with no fragment component, so an entry the grammar refuses (a `userinfo` in an `https` value, a host that is not ASCII) is carried, as the seam spelled it and never folded; when any entry fails the rule nothing is carried and the refresh token holds no granted set. [RFC 8693 §2.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8693#section-2.1) defines `audience` as "The logical name of the target service where the client intends to use the requested security token", so the granted set is deduplicated by exact spelling and not by comparison key: a resource indicator is folded before it is stored, so its exact spelling and its comparison key agree, while two carried audiences that differ only in the case of the scheme or the host are two names, both issued by a refresh that names no `resource`; a request's `resource` is folded at its ingress (`ReadResource`), so a refresh naming either spelling arrives as the folded one and is answered with the carried audience stored in the folded spelling when there is one and otherwise with the first carried audience with its comparison key, and the carried audience stored in the other spelling is issued only by a refresh that names no `resource`.
  - **The access token's audience.** [RFC 9068 §3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9068#section-3): "If the request includes a "resource" parameter (as defined in [RFC8707]), the resulting JWT access token "aud" claim SHOULD have the same value as the "resource" parameter in the request." The issued `aud` carries the granted entry's stored spelling, which is the folded one (the comparison above), so a request that spells its `resource` with capitals in the scheme or host receives an `aud` in another spelling than its own: a deviation from that SHOULD, named below, which follows from the mapping [RFC 8707 §2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8707#section-2) permits. "If the request does not include a "resource" parameter, the authorization server MUST use a default resource indicator in the "aud" claim. If a "scope" parameter is present in the request, the authorization server SHOULD use it to infer the value of the default resource indicator to be used in the "aud" claim." A request that names no `resource` takes its default from the application's audience resolver, `ResolveAccessTokenAudienceDelegate` (`AuthorizationServerIntegration.ResolveAccessTokenAudienceAsync`), whose library default `Rfc9068AccessTokenProducer.DefaultResolveAccessTokenAudienceAsync` reads `ClientRecord.ScopeToAudience` for the granted scopes, under the deployment's `AccessTokenAudPolicy`: `Required` refuses to issue without an audience, while `Optional` issues no `aud` when the resolver answers none and `Suppressed` never issues one, each a deviation of that policy from the MUST, named below. "If the values in the "scope" parameter refer to different default resource indicator values, the authorization server SHOULD reject the request with "invalid_scope" as described in Section 4.1.2.1 of [RFC6749]." The default resolver answers the union of the granted scopes' audiences instead, a deviation of the default named below; an application that wants the refusal supplies its own resolver. "The authorization server MUST NOT issue a JWT access token if the authorization granted by the token would be ambiguous." and [§5](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9068#section-5)'s "The details on how to recognize and mitigate this and other ambiguous situations is highly scenario dependent and hence is out of scope for this profile." are the application's: its resolver, its `ScopeToAudience` and its authorization seams decide which scopes and audiences one token carries.
  - **The application's turn.** The library owns the syntax, the fold and the key; the policy is the application's. [RFC 8707 §2.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8707#section-2.1): "the authorization server MAY process the request with no specific resource or by using a predefined default resource value. Alternatively, the authorization server MAY require clients to specify the resource(s) they intend to access and MAY fail requests that omit the parameter with an "invalid_target" error." [§2.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8707#section-2.2): "To the extent possible, when issuing access tokens, the authorization server should downscope the scope value associated with an access token to the value the respective resource is able to process and needs to know." [§3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8707#section-3): "an authorization server may be unwilling or unable to fulfill a token request with multiple resources." [RFC 8693 §2.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8693#section-2.1): "audience" values "must be unique within that server to ensure that they are properly interpreted as the intended type of value." [RFC 9110 §4.2.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110#section-4.2.3): "distinct resources SHOULD NOT be identified by HTTP URIs that are equivalent after normalization (using any method defined in Section 6.2 of [URI])." A default or required resource, the downscoping, the refusal of several resources, the uniqueness of audience values and any identity between two indicators beyond the fold are decisions of the application's seams: `EvaluateAuthorizationRequestDelegate` receives the folded values as `AuthorizationRequestEvaluation.RequestedResource` and answers `AuthorizationDenialReason.InvalidTarget`, which the library maps to `invalid_target`; `AuthorizeTokenExchangeAsync` receives the folded `resource` values (an `audience` value is a logical name and reaches it as sent) and a denial naming an audience answers `invalid_target`.
  - **The client's turn.** The library's request builders carry the application's `resource` value to the wire without folding it: `TokenExchangeRequestBuilder` refuses an entry that the shared resource-indicator grammar refuses and sends every other entry as its own occurrence, unfolded, and the request-object start options refuse an entry holding white space, and nothing else is checked. The client-side clauses are the application's: the [MCP authorization](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2026-07-28/basic/authorization#resource-parameter-implementation) `resource` parameter "MUST be included in both authorization requests and token requests", "MUST identify the MCP server that the client intends to use the token with" and "MUST use the canonical URI of the MCP server"; [RFC 8707 §2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8707#section-2) "The client SHOULD provide the most specific URI that it can for the complete API or set of resources it intends to access." and "The client SHOULD use the base URI of the API as the "resource" parameter value unless specific knowledge of the resource dictates otherwise."; [RFC 8707 §3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8707#section-3) "Whenever feasible, the "resource" parameter should correspond to the network-addressable location of the protected resource." and, for an abstract identifier, "it is the client's responsibility to validate out of band that any network endpoint to which tokens are sent are the intended audience for that identifier."; [RFC 9110 §4.2.4](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110#section-4.2.4) "A sender MUST NOT generate the userinfo subcomponent (and its "@" delimiter) when an "http" or "https" URI reference is generated within a message as a target URI or field value."; and the sender MUST NOTs of [§4.2.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110#section-4.2.1) and [§4.2.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110#section-4.2.2) against an empty host. The application owns the canonical form it sends.
  - **Deviations.** Each is named with its reason.
