    - The token exchange answers a malformed `resource` with `invalid_target`, a deviation from [RFC 8693 §2.2.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8693#section-2.2.2)'s "If the request itself is not valid" and "The value of the "error" parameter MUST be the "invalid_request" error code." The reason is [RFC 8707 §2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8707#section-2), which provides the error code for an access token request ("The following error code is provided for an authorization server to indicate problems with the requested resource(s) in response to an authorization request or access token request."), together with the authorization request's instruction of [RFC 8707 §2.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8707#section-2.1) for a value the server fails to parse, which this library applies at the token endpoint as its own reading, and RFC 8693 Section 2.2.2's "the "invalid_target" error code SHOULD be used in the error response" for a target service the server is unable to issue for: this library reads the two as one error code per parameter at every endpoint, the token exchange included.
    - Repeated `resource` parameters are aggregated, a deviation from [RFC 6749 §3.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6749#section-3.1)'s "Request and response parameters MUST NOT be included more than once." The reason is that [RFC 8707 §2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8707#section-2) is the rule for this parameter and supersedes the general one: "Multiple "resource" parameters MAY be used to indicate that the requested token is intended to be used at multiple resources."
    - A `resource` carrying a query component is accepted, a deviation from [RFC 8707 §2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8707#section-2)'s "It SHOULD NOT include a query component". The clause itself recognizes "that there are cases that make a query component a useful and necessary part of the resource parameter, such as when one or more query parameters are used to scope requests to an application", and the library accepts the value the client names.
    - Only the scheme and the host are normalized, a deviation from the further normalizations [RFC 3986 §6.2.2.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc3986#section-6.2.2.1) ("the hexadecimal digits within a percent-encoding triplet (e.g., "%3a" versus "%3A") are case-insensitive and therefore should be normalized to use uppercase letters for the digits A-F"), [§6.2.2.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc3986#section-6.2.2.2) ("These URIs should be normalized by decoding any percent-encoded octet that corresponds to an unreserved character"), [§6.2.2.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc3986#section-6.2.2.3) ("URI normalizers should remove dot-segments by applying the remove_dot_segments algorithm to the path") and [§6.2.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc3986#section-6.2.3) ("a URI that uses the generic syntax for authority with an empty path should be normalized to a path of "/"" and an explicit port "for which the port is empty or the default for the scheme" "should be removed") describe, that [RFC 9110 §4.2.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110#section-4.2.3) repeats for `http` and `https` (omit the default port, provide a path of "/", "Characters other than those in the "reserved" set are equivalent to their percent-encoded octets"), and that the [MCP authorization](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2026-07-28/basic/authorization#canonical-server-uri) note asks for ("implementations SHOULD consistently use the form without the trailing slash"). None is applied and every other spelling is compared exactly and refused when it differs, so `https://x` and `https://x/`, `%2f` and `%2F`, `:443` and no port, and `/a/../b` and `/b` are different indicators, and a token request naming one spelling of a granted pair is refused `invalid_target` as not granted (`NarrowingRefusesAnEquivalentSpellingTheLibraryDoesNotNormalizeAsync`). The reason is fail closed: an equivalent spelling is refused, never widened into a grant or an audience the resource owner did not name, and [RFC 3986 §6.2.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc3986#section-6.2.1) states that identical strings are equivalent.
    - For every scheme other than `http` and `https`, the `userinfo` the generic syntax admits is accepted, stored and minted into `aud` as sent, the deprecated `user:password` form included: a deviation from [RFC 3986 §3.2.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc3986#section-3.2.1)'s "should reject the storage of such data in unencrypted form". The reason is that the generic grammar admits it and no rule of RFC 8707 forbids it. [§7.6](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc3986#section-7.6) names the look-alike hazard, a `userinfo` "appearing to identify one (trusted) naming authority while actually identifying a different authority hidden behind the noise", which the library refuses where [RFC 9110 §4.2.4](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110#section-4.2.4) says to (`http` and `https`) and leaves to the application's seams for every other scheme.
    - The choice [RFC 8707 §2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8707#section-2) leaves ("may use the exact "resource" value as the audience or it may map from that value") is taken as a mapping: the fold is applied before storage, so a stored grant holds the folded spelling. This is a permitted choice and not a departure.
    - A stored grant value the grammar refuses (an IRI, or any other value outside the ASCII grammar, such as a carried audience the grammar refuses) is compared as itself by `ComparisonKey` and applies only to a token request that names no resource, so it can never share a comparison key with a value the request supplies.
    - A malformed `resource` claim of a signed ID-JAG is answered `invalid_grant` ([RFC 7523 §3.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7523#section-3.1)), not the `invalid_target` that [RFC 8707 §2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8707#section-2) registers for a resource that is "invalid, missing, unknown, or malformed", although [draft-ietf-oauth-identity-assertion-authz-grant-04 §4.4.1](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-ietf-oauth-identity-assertion-authz-grant-04#section-4.4.1) processes the claim "according to Section 2 of [RFC8707]". The reason: the claim is part of a signed grant, so a grant carrying a malformed one is a JWT that is not valid ("If the JWT is not valid, or the current time is not within the token's valid time window for use, the authorization server constructs an error response as defined in OAuth 2.0 [RFC6749]. The value of the "error" parameter MUST be the "invalid_grant" error code."), not a request parameter RFC 8707 Section 2 answers with `invalid_target` (`IdJagValidationFailureReason.MalformedResource`).
    - An ID-JAG `resource` claim that is the empty string or the empty array is refused as `MalformedResource`. [Draft-04 §3.1](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-ietf-oauth-identity-assertion-authz-grant-04#section-3.1) names the claim's shapes, "(either a single URI or an array of URIs)", and no sentence of Section 3.1 or Section 4.4.1 refuses an array of none or an empty string; the refusal is this library's fail-closed reading. The reason: such a claim names no resource server, and read as absent it would let the grant reach the default audience the issuer did not name (`IdJagValidationFailureReason.MalformedResource`).
    - The access token's `aud` carries the granted entry's folded spelling where the request named another spelling of the same indicator, a deviation from [RFC 9068 §3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9068#section-3)'s "the resulting JWT access token "aud" claim SHOULD have the same value as the "resource" parameter in the request". The reason: the fold is applied before storage (the permitted mapping above), and one spelling per resource server is what a resource server compares its audience against.
    - Under `AccessTokenAudPolicy.Optional` an access token whose resolver answers no audience is issued without `aud`, and under `AccessTokenAudPolicy.Suppressed` every access token is, a deviation from [RFC 9068 §3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9068#section-3)'s "the authorization server MUST use a default resource indicator in the "aud" claim". The reason, in `AccessTokenAudPolicy`: the two values are the deployment's explicit choice for resource servers that do not enforce the claim; `Required` follows the MUST.
    - The default audience resolver answers the union of the audiences the granted scopes map to, where [RFC 9068 §3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9068#section-3) reads "If the values in the "scope" parameter refer to different default resource indicator values, the authorization server SHOULD reject the request with "invalid_scope" as described in Section 4.1.2.1 of [RFC6749]." The reason: which scopes may share one token is the application's policy; an application that wants the refusal supplies its own `ResolveAccessTokenAudienceDelegate`.
  - Proven by `Rfc8707ResourceIndicatorTests`: the grammar and its refusals at the pushed request, `PushedAuthorizationRequestRejectsNonAbsoluteResourceWithInvalidTarget`, `PushedAuthorizationRequestRejectsResourceWithFragmentWithInvalidTarget`, `PushedAuthorizationRequestRejectsRelativeResourceWithInvalidTarget`, `ANonAsciiResourceIndicatorIsRefusedAtThePushedRequestAsync`, `AResourceIndicatorOutsideTheRfc3986GrammarIsRefusedAtThePushedRequestAsync`, `AnHttpIndicatorWithoutAHostIsRefusedAsync`, `AResourceIndicatorWithAnEmptySchemeIsRefusedAtThePushedRequestAndTheClientCredentialsGrantAsync` and `AnHttpIndicatorWithUserInformationIsRefusedAsync`; the other receipts, `DirectAuthorizeRejectsMalformedResourceWithInvalidTargetRedirect`, `JarParRejectsMalformedResourceClaimWithInvalidTarget`, `JarByValueRejectsMalformedResourceClaimWithInvalidTargetRedirect`, `ARequestObjectResourceArrayWithAMalformedElementIsRefusedAsync`, `ClientCredentialsRejectsMalformedResourceWithInvalidTarget`, `ANonAsciiResourceIndicatorIsRefusedAtEveryOtherReceiptAsync`, `ANonAsciiResourceAtRefreshIsRefusedBeforeCorrelationAsync`, `DirectAuthorizeOverTheWireDoesNotRedirectToAnUnregisteredRedirectUriWithANonAsciiResourceAsync` and `AWhitespaceBearingResourceIsRefusedWithItsFailingPartAsync`; the trace event, `AMalformedResourceIsRecordedOnTheRequestTraceByItsFailingPartAsync`; the redirect's description, `AnApplicationDenialDescriptionReachesTheRedirectInsideTheRfc6749CharsetAsync` (the plain branch) and `AnApplicationDenialDescriptionInASignedResponseIsInsideTheRfc6749CharsetAsync` (the JARM branch); the query component accepted, `PushedAuthorizationRequestAcceptsResourceWithQueryComponent`; empty as omitted, `EmptyResourceValueAtPushedAuthorizationRequestIsOmitted`, `AnEmptyResourceValueIsOmittedAtEveryAuthorizationFormReceiptAsync`, `EmptyResourceValueAtCodeRedemptionIsOmitted`, `EmptyResourceValueIsOmittedAtCodeRedemptionOverRealWireAsync`, `EmptyResourceValueAtRefreshIsOmitted`, `EmptyResourceValueIsOmittedAtRefreshOverRealWireAsync`, `ClientCredentialsTreatsAnEmptyResourceValueAsOmitted` and `ClientCredentialsTreatsAnEmptyResourceValueAsOmittedOverTheWireAsync`; an empty occurrence beside a valid one dropped, `PushedAuthorizationRequestDropsAnEmptyResourceOccurrenceMixedWithValidOne`, `AnEmptyResourceOccurrenceMixedWithValidOneIsDroppedAtEveryAuthorizationFormReceiptAsync`, `EmptyResourceOccurrenceMixedWithValidOneNarrowsAtCodeRedemptionOverRealWireAsync`, `EmptyResourceOccurrenceMixedWithValidOneNarrowsAtRefreshOverRealWireAsync`, `ClientCredentialsDropsAnEmptyResourceOccurrenceMixedWithValidOne` and `ClientCredentialsDropsAnEmptyResourceOccurrenceMixedWithValidOneOverTheWireAsync`; and a whitespace-only occurrence malformed, `WhitespaceResourceValueIsMalformedAtCodeRedemption`, `WhitespaceResourceValueIsMalformedAtCodeRedemptionOverRealWireAsync`, `WhitespaceResourceValueIsMalformedAtRefresh` and `WhitespaceResourceValueIsMalformedAtRefreshOverRealWireAsync`; repetition, `RepeatedResourceParametersAggregateAtDirectAuthorize`, `RepeatedResourceParametersNarrowAtCodeRedemption` and `ClientCredentialsWithRepeatedResourceProducesArrayAudience`; the fold and the comparison key, `AnUppercaseSchemeAndHostResourceIsGrantedTheCanonicalAudienceAsync`, `NarrowingMatchesAGrantedResourceByItsKeyAsync`, `NarrowingRefusesAResourceThatDiffersOnlyInTheCaseOfAKeyedPartAsync`, `NarrowingRefusesAnEquivalentSpellingTheLibraryDoesNotNormalizeAsync`, `PathQueryAndPercentEncodingKeepTheirSpellingAsync`, `OnlyTheSchemeAndHostOfAnAuthorityAreFoldedAsync`, `TheSchemeOfAnIndicatorWithoutAnAuthorityIsFoldedAsync` and `AnIdnHostInItsALabelFormIsAcceptedAndFoldedAsync`; the subset rule, `CodeRedemptionWithSubsetRequestResourceNarrowsAud`, `CodeRedemptionWithResourceNotInGrantedSetIsRefusedInvalidTarget`, `RefreshWithSubsetResourceNarrowsAud` and `RefreshTokenStaysBoundToFullGrantAcrossANarrowedRefresh`; the application's denial, `ApplicationDenialWithInvalidTargetReasonProducesInvalidTargetError`. `ResourceIndicatorSyntaxTests`: `FoldLowersOnlyTheAsciiLettersOfTheSchemeAndHost` (a hexadecimal IP-literal host lowered; a `userinfo` in the `user:password` form, a port and a percent-encoded octet kept as sent), `CheckRefusesEveryCodePointOutsideAscii`, `CheckNamesTheFirstPartThatFails` (the empty scheme refused; the `userinfo` of `ftp://user@host/` accepted), `CheckReadsEveryIpLiteralForm`, `ComparisonKeyFoldsAnAcceptedIndicatorAndKeepsAnyOtherValue` and `FoldReturnsAValueTheCheckRefusesUnchanged`. `TokenExchangeGrantTests`: `MalformedResourceIsRejectedFailClosed` (the `invalid_target` deviation), `TokenExchangeResourceIsFoldedAtIngressAsync`, `ANonAsciiResourceIsRefusedAtTheTokenExchangeAsync`, `AWhitespaceBearingResourceIsRefusedAtTheTokenExchangeAsync`, `RepeatedResourceCarriesEveryTargetIntoTheAuthorizationSeam`, `TokenExchangeTreatsAnEmptyResourceValueAsOmitted`, `TokenExchangeDropsAnEmptyResourceOccurrenceMixedWithValidOne`, and for the `audience`, `AnAudienceParameterReachesTheAuthorizationSeamAndTheIssuedTokenUnfoldedAsync`, `AnExchangeAuthorizedAudienceKeepsItsSpellingThroughRefreshAndRotationAsync`, `TwoAuthorizedAudiencesThatShareAComparisonKeyStayTwoThroughRefreshAsync`, `TokenExchangeTreatsAnEmptyAudienceValueAsOmitted`, `TokenExchangeDropsAnEmptyAudienceOccurrenceMixedWithValidOne`, `TokenExchangeRefusesAWhitespaceAudienceOccurrenceAsInvalidRequest` and `PolicyDenialWithNamedAudienceIsInvalidTarget`. `VcalmConformanceBridgeTests`, the resource server's exact audience comparison: `CaseVariantResourceIndicatorIsIssuedThisInstancesAudience`, `AudienceTrailingSlashIsInvalidToken` and `AudienceDefaultPortIsInvalidToken`. `RefreshGrantTests`, the scope refusal's fixed description: `RefreshWithAnUngrantedScopeValueIsRefusedInvalidScopeWithoutConsumingTheTokenOverHttpWire`. The ID-JAG's `resource` claim at the redeem: `IdJagAssertionValidationTests.ResourceArrayWithANonStringElementIsMalformedResource`, `AResourceClaimOutsideTheGrammarIsMalformedResource` (the userinfo case under RFC 9110 Section 4.2.4, the empty string and the empty array the fail-closed reading, and a number, a boolean, an object, a JSON `null` and an array holding a `null`), `AResourceClaimIsFoldedAtTheRedeem`, and the pins `AbsentResourceClaimSurfacesEmpty`, `ResourceStringClaimIsSurfaced` and `ResourceArrayClaimIsSurfaced`; over the listener under the "tr-TR" culture, decoded by the library's own `JwtPartJson.Default`, `IdJagGrantTests.AnIdJagResourceClaimIsFoldedAtTheRedeemAsync` (the access token's `aud` folded) and `AMalformedIdJagResourceClaimIsRefusedAtTheRedeemAsync` (`invalid_grant`, a JSON `null` and an array holding a `null` included, the application's association never called and called once for a valid companion grant). The grant's own `aud` compared as signed: `IdJagAssertionValidationTests.AnAudienceDifferingFromTheIssuerOnlyInCaseIsAudienceMismatch` and `AnIssuerSpelledWithCapitalsAcceptsTheSameAudienceAsSigned`, and over the listener `IdJagGrantTests.AnIdJagAudienceDifferingFromTheIssuerOnlyInCaseIsRefusedAtTheRedeemAsync` and `AnIssuerSpelledWithCapitalsRedeemsTheSameAudienceAsSignedAsync`. The mint's refusal of a granted `resource` the grammar refuses: `IdJagGrantTests.MintRefusesAResourceTheGrammarRefusesFromTheSeamAsync`. The access token's audience: `IdJagGrantTests.ARedeemNamingNoResourceUnderTwoMappedScopesIssuesTheUnionAsync` (the default resolver's union over the listener), `AuthorizationServerFeatureTests.DefaultResolverReturnsNullWhenScopeToAudienceUnset`, `DefaultResolverMapsSingleScopeToAudiences` and `DefaultResolverDedupesAcrossScopes`, `Rfc8707ResourceIndicatorTests.SuppressedAudPolicyOmitsAudEvenWithValidatedResource` (`Suppressed`), `Rfc8707ResourceIndicatorTests.ClientCredentialsTreatsAnEmptyResourceValueAsOmittedOverTheWireAsync` (`Optional`: a request with no audience resolved is issued no `aud`, over the listener), and `IdJagGrantTests.ARedeemThatGrantsAResourceAndAScopeAnswersBothAsync` (the granted resource as the access token's `aud`).

- **The ID-JAG `sub_id` is any RFC 9493 Subject Identifier at the mint and is judged under a rule at the redeem.** [draft-ietf-oauth-identity-assertion-authz-grant-04 §3.1](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-ietf-oauth-identity-assertion-authz-grant-04#section-3.1) (draft-04 below) defines "sub_id: OPTIONAL - Subject Identifier as defined in [RFC9493]." and states "When both sub and sub_id are present, they MUST identify the same End-User." The mint writes the claim only from the identifier the authorization seam chooses, and the redeem compares it with the grant's validated `iss`, `tenant` and `sub` when its rule asks for a comparison. [draft-04 §3.2.2](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-ietf-oauth-identity-assertion-authz-grant-04#section-3.2.2): "If the Resource Authorization Server requires a SAML NameID Subject Identifier for subject resolution, it MUST reject the token request with an invalid_grant error as defined in Section 5.2 of [RFC6749] if any of the following is true", "the ID-JAG does not contain a sub_id claim using the SAML NameID Subject Identifier Format", "the sub_id claim is malformed or uses a Subject Identifier Format that the Resource Authorization Server does not support", and "the sub_id claim is not authorized by local policy for the validated ID-JAG issuer." A rule whose presence is `IdJagSubjectIdentifierPresence.Required` makes the three refusals, each answered `invalid_grant`. [draft-04 §9.5](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-ietf-oauth-identity-assertion-authz-grant-04#section-9.5): "When the ID-JAG includes a sub_id claim, the Resource Authorization Server MUST NOT use the sub_id.issuer value to establish trust in the ID-JAG issuer." The library reads the claim after every other claim rule, on a grant whose signature and issuer the application's redeem seam has verified, and derives no trust from it. [draft-04 §9.6](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-ietf-oauth-identity-assertion-authz-grant-04#section-9.6): "The IdP Authorization Server SHOULD include sub_id only when needed by the target Resource Authorization Server for subject resolution." The grant carries no `sub_id` unless the seam returns one. [RFC 9493 §3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9493#section-3): "A Subject Identifier MUST NOT contain any members prohibited or not described by its Identifier Format and MUST contain all members required by its Identifier Format." The one check the mint and the redeem read holds every format the catalog knows to exactly the members that format describes.
  - **The identifier.** Core's `SubjectIdentifier` carries a `Format` and its `Members`; `SubjectIdentifier.FromWireObject` reads a JSON object whose `format` member is a non-empty string and answers `null` for any other object. `SubjectIdentifierFormats.IsKnown` names the thirteen formats the catalog holds, compared ordinally: the eight of [RFC 9493 §3.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9493#section-3.2) (`account`, `email`, `iss_sub`, `opaque`, `phone_number`, `did`, `uri`, `aliases`), the four of [OpenID Shared Signals Framework 1.0 §3.3](https://openid.net/specs/openid-sharedsignals-framework-1_0.html#section-3.3) and [§3.5](https://openid.net/specs/openid-sharedsignals-framework-1_0.html#section-3.5) (`jwt_id`, `saml_assertion_id`, `ip-addresses`, `complex`), and `saml-nameid` of [draft-04 §3.2.1](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-ietf-oauth-identity-assertion-authz-grant-04#section-3.2.1). `SubjectIdentifier.IsValidForKnownFormat` answers `true` only for a format the catalog knows whose member rules hold. An identifier of a format of RFC 9493 Section 3.2 other than `aliases`, of `saml-nameid`, of `jwt_id` or of `saml_assertion_id` carries exactly the members its format describes, each required member a non-empty JSON string and each optional member of `saml-nameid` a JSON string. An `ip-addresses` identifier carries its one member as a JSON array of one or more non-empty strings, and a JSON string in its place is refused. An `aliases` identifier carries its one `identifiers` member as a JSON array of one or more Subject Identifiers, none of them `aliases`, the letter of [RFC 9493 §3.2.8](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9493#section-3.2.8): ""aliases" Subject Identifiers MUST NOT be nested, i.e., the "identifiers" member of an "aliases" Subject Identifier MUST NOT contain a Subject Identifier in the Aliases Identifier Format." The text forbids no other element, so a `complex` subject is admitted as one. A `complex` subject carries one or more members, each a simple subject or an `aliases` identifier, none of them `complex`. The two formats nest each other, and the nesting is bounded at a depth of 32, this library's resource rule (a deviation below): the identifier checked stands at depth one, each `aliases` element and `complex` member one level deeper, and an identifier holding one deeper than 32 is neither valid nor well formed; the bound counts identifiers, not JSON levels. One check walks a nested object once for each depth it needs: it remembers, by reference, each nested object it found well formed and the deepest depth it found it at, and walks it again only where it stands deeper, since no rule but the bound depends on the depth; the work of one check is linear in the size of the identifier's distinct nested objects, their members and elements, times the bound, and a writer of the identifier writes every path, since JSON carries no references. An `aliases` element or a `complex` member of a format the catalog knows satisfies that format, and one of a format the catalog does not know is admitted. No identifier carries a `Members` entry named `format`, and a blank `Format` names no format at any depth. `SubjectIdentifier.IsWellFormed` reads the same rules and differs in one answer: an identifier of a format the catalog does not know is well formed and carried opaque, since [RFC 9493 §3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9493#section-3) reads "Every Identifier Format MUST have a unique name registered in the IANA "Security Event Identifier Formats" registry established in Section 8.1 or a Collision-Resistant Name as defined in [RFC7519]." The mint and the redeem read `IsWellFormed`. The Global Token Revocation endpoint reads `IsValidForKnownFormat` and answers any other `sub_id` 400 `invalid_request` before its revoke seam is called, and `SamlNameIdSubjectIdentifier.TryFrom` reads `IsValidForKnownFormat` for the `saml-nameid` projection.
  - **The mint.** `TokenExchangeAuthorization.SubjectIdentifier` is the authorization seam's choice of identifier for the End-User the grant's `sub` names; `null`, the default, omits the claim. `AuthCodeEndpoints.BuildIdJagMintResponseAsync` checks the chosen identifier after the capability gate, the audience and the resource client identifier and before it generates the grant's `jti` and resolves the signing material: an identifier that is not well formed, a `TokenExchangeAuthorization.Resource` entry that `ResourceIndicatorSyntax.IsAbsoluteResourceIndicator` refuses ([draft-04 §3.1](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-ietf-oauth-identity-assertion-authz-grant-04#section-3.1): "resource: OPTIONAL - The Resource Identifier (Section 2 of [RFC8707]) of the Resource Server (either a single URI or an array of URIs)."), a non-empty `TokenExchangeAuthorization.Scope` that breaks the grammar of [RFC 6749 §3.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6749#section-3.3) ([draft-04 §3.1](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-ietf-oauth-identity-assertion-authz-grant-04#section-3.1): "scope: OPTIONAL - a JSON string containing a space-separated list of scopes associated with the token, in the format described in Section 3.3 of [RFC6749]."), or a `TokenExchangeAuthorization.Tenant`, `AudienceTenant` or `AudienceSubject` of white space only, is answered `server_error` with `WellKnownOAuthErrorDescriptions.ServerNotReady` and nothing is minted; a tenant relationship value that is null or empty omits its claim, as an empty scope does. The grammar is checked by one internal member, `ScopeTokenSyntax.IsScopeList`, of an internal class of its own, which the mint, the redeem and `BearerTokenChallenge.BuildChallenge` read. A well-formed identifier is written as `SubjectIdentifier.ToWireObject` renders it, one of a format the catalog does not know included. A SAML NameID identifier comes from `SamlNameIdSubjectIdentifier.ToSubjectIdentifier`, which writes each optional member exactly when it is present. `sub_id` is one of the claim names the mint reserves, so a `sub_id` among `TokenExchangeAuthorization.AdditionalClaims` never reaches the grant.
  - **The redeem.** `IdJagSubjectIdentifierRule` carries `Presence` (`Optional` by default, or `Required`), `AcceptedFormats` (empty by default, which accepts every format; the init accessor keeps an ordinal snapshot of the set it is given), `Relation` (`null` by default, which asks for no comparison under `Optional`) and `Association`, the application's `CompareDelegate<SubjectIdentifier, IdJagSubjectContext>`. `IdJagAssertionValidation.ValidateAsync` applies the rule it is given; `IdJagAssertionValidation.Validate` applies `IdJagSubjectIdentifierRule.Default`. The steps run in this order: the [draft-04 §4.4.1](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-ietf-oauth-identity-assertion-authz-grant-04#section-4.4.1) and [§9.3](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-ietf-oauth-identity-assertion-authz-grant-04#section-9.3) rules (the `typ` header, `iss`, the trust-domain rule, `aud`, `client_id`, `sub`, the time claims) and the reads of `cnf`, `act` and `may_act`; then the tenant relationship claims, each of `tenant`, `aud_tenant` and `aud_sub` refused when it is present and is not a JSON string with at least one character that is not white space (`MalformedTenant`, `MalformedAudienceTenant`, `MalformedAudienceSubject`); then `scope`, `jti`, `resource` and `authorization_details`; then the `sub_id` claim. The time claims are read by the shared `Rfc7523AssertionValidation` as the NumericDate of [RFC 7519 §2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7519#section-2), "A JSON numeric value representing the number of seconds from 1970-01-01T00:00:00Z UTC until the specified UTC date/time, ignoring leap seconds." where "non-integer values can be represented": the fraction of an `exp`, `nbf` or `iat` is kept to the tick, and a number that names no instant a `DateTimeOffset` holds, or one beyond a 64-bit integer, is a NumericDate this server cannot represent, refused as `InconsistentTemporalClaims` with a description that says so (this library's fail-closed reading, a deviation below). A time claim that is a JSON string, digits included, is no number and is refused as `InconsistentTemporalClaims` with the description of a time claim that is not a numeric timestamp: [RFC 7519 §4.1.4](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7519#section-4.1.4), [§4.1.5](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7519#section-4.1.5) and [§4.1.6](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7519#section-4.1.6) each read "Its value MUST be a number containing a NumericDate value." Every comparison of a time claim with the clock and its skew is made through the difference of the two instants, so an `exp` within the skew of the last representable instant is not expired, an `nbf` at the first representable instant is in force, and none of these checks throws. The replay records the token endpoint keeps, for the grant's `jti` and for the `jti` of a `private_key_jwt` client assertion under the replay delegate of `PrivateKeyJwtClientAuthentication.BuildValidator`, end at the last representable instant when the expiry and the tolerance pass it. The client assertion's readers on the same request, `PrivateKeyJwtClientAuthentication` and `FederationClientAuthentication`, read their time claims through the same internal reader, `Rfc7523AssertionValidation.ReadNumericDate`, which refuses a JSON string as the redeem does, and compare them the same way. [Draft-04 §3.1](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-ietf-oauth-identity-assertion-authz-grant-04#section-3.1) makes two claims REQUIRED that RFC 7523 leaves OPTIONAL, "jti: REQUIRED - Unique ID of this JWT as defined in Section 4.1.7 of [RFC7519]." and "iat: REQUIRED - as defined in Section 4.1.6 of [RFC7519].": a grant whose time claims hold without an `iat` is `MissingIssuedAt` (a present `iat` that is not numeric keeps the time claims' `InconsistentTemporalClaims`), and a grant without a `jti` that is a JSON string with at least one character that is not white space is `MissingJti`, so a successful result always carries `Jti` and `IssuedAt`. [Draft-04 §4.4.1](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-ietf-oauth-identity-assertion-authz-grant-04#section-4.4.1): "If the scope claim is present, the Resource Authorization Server MUST process it according to Section 3.3 of [RFC6749]." [RFC 6749 §3.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6749#section-3.3) gives the grammar, "scope = scope-token *( SP scope-token )" and "scope-token = 1*( %x21 / %x23-5B / %x5D-7E )": a present `scope` claim that is not a JSON string, or whose value breaks the grammar (the empty string, a leading, trailing or doubled space, a tab, a character outside the set), is `MalformedScope`, and a valid one is surfaced as `IdJagAssertionValidationResult.Scope`. **Open.** The library's JWT part decoder, `JwtPartJson.Default`, reads a JSON string claim shaped like a date as a date, so under it the claim core refuses every date-shaped string claim its conversion reaches: a `jti` (`MissingJti`), a `scope` (`MalformedScope`), a `sub` (`MissingSubject`), a `client_id` (`MissingClientId`), a `tenant` (`MalformedTenant`), an `aud_tenant` (`MalformedAudienceTenant`), an `aud_sub` (`MalformedAudienceSubject`) and a member of a `sub_id` claim the rule judges (`MalformedSubjectIdentifier`), a scope such as `2026-10-01` that the grammar accepts and a subject or tenant such as `2026-10-01` included; the decoder's correction is a separate change. A present `resource` claim is checked against the resource indicator grammar and surfaced folded, and is otherwise `MalformedResource` (the gates of the resource indicators entry). [Draft-04 §4.4.1](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-ietf-oauth-identity-assertion-authz-grant-04#section-4.4.1): "If the authorization_details claim is present, the Resource Authorization Server MUST parse it as a JSON array and process each authorization detail object according to [RFC9396]." A present claim that is not a JSON array is `MalformedAuthorizationDetails`; the shape of each authorization detail object is the application's to process. The duties of [draft-04 §4.4.1](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-ietf-oauth-identity-assertion-authz-grant-04#section-4.4.1) the library does not perform are the application's, which decides the grant from the surfaced `IdJagAssertionValidationResult.Resource`, `IdJagAssertionValidationResult.Scope` and `IdJagAssertionValidationResult.AuthorizationDetails`: "If both resource and authorization_details claims are present, the Resource Authorization Server MUST process both. The Resource Authorization Server SHOULD ensure consistency between the resource identifiers and authorization details when issuing the access token." and "If both scope and authorization_details claims are present, the Resource Authorization Server MUST process both. The Resource Authorization Server SHOULD ensure consistency between the scopes and authorization details when issuing the access token.", with the policy choices of the same section, which resources, scopes and authorization details to grant. The response duty, "The Resource Authorization Server MUST include the granted resource (if any), scope (if any), and authorization_details (if any) in the access token response. The response format follows Section 2 of [RFC8707] for resource, Section 5.1 of [RFC6749] for scope, and Section 2.2 of [RFC9396] for authorization_details.", is read this way: [RFC 8707 §2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8707#section-2) defines no response member, so the granted resource is carried as the access token's `aud`; the granted scope is always the response's `scope` member, which covers [RFC 6749 §5.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc6749#section-5.1)'s "OPTIONAL, if identical to the scope requested by the client; otherwise, REQUIRED."; and `authorization_details` is the response's member when the application supplies the response value. The statements of [draft-04 §3.1](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-ietf-oauth-identity-assertion-authz-grant-04#section-3.1) the library does not check are the application's as well: "The combination of aud + aud_tenant and aud_sub MUST be unique within the Resource Authorization Server." binds the Resource Authorization Server's own subject resolution over the surfaced `Audience`, `AudienceTenant` and `AudienceSubject`; the RECOMMENDED `email` and/or `aud_sub` claim of the grant is the minting application's, satisfied through the authorization seam's `TokenExchangeAuthorization.AudienceSubject` and `TokenExchangeAuthorization.AdditionalClaims`; the two MAYs of the same paragraph, "The Resource Authorization Server MAY use these claims for subject resolution, including JIT provisioning, for example when the user has not yet SSO'd into the Resource Authorization Server." and "Additional Resource Authorization Server specific identity claims MAY be needed for subject resolution.", are the redeeming application's, which resolves the subject from the payload through its association and its redeem seam; and the OPTIONAL `auth_time`, `acr`, `amr` and `email` claims are left on the payload for the application, which the validator neither reads nor surfaces. At the `sub_id` step, an absent claim under `Required` is `SubjectIdentifierRequired`. At the `sub_id` step, a present claim that is not a well-formed Subject Identifier is `MalformedSubjectIdentifier` when the rule requires the claim or names a `Relation`, and is surfaced as `null` otherwise. At the `sub_id` step, a claim of a format outside a non-empty `AcceptedFormats` under `Required` is `UnsupportedSubjectIdentifierFormat`. At the `sub_id` step, when the rule requires the claim or names a `Relation`, the claim is then compared: a `Deny` is `SubjectIdentifierMismatch`, and any other decision than `Permit` is `SubjectIdentifierUndetermined`. The application's `ValidateJwtBearerAssertionDelegate` is to return no grant for a failed result, and the token endpoint answers every reason with one body, `invalid_grant` with the description `The JWT assertion is not valid.`. A successful result surfaces `IdJagAssertionValidationResult.SubjectIdentifier` (the well-formed claim, one of a format the catalog does not know carried opaque), `IdJagAssertionValidationResult.SamlNameIdSubjectIdentifier` (its projection through `SamlNameIdSubjectIdentifier.TryFrom` when its format is `saml-nameid`) and `IdJagAssertionValidationResult.SubjectIdentifierComparison`, the `ComparisonRecord` of the comparison, which also rides the two refusals the comparison decides; a `null` record means nothing judged the claim. The text of `IdJagAssertionValidationResult`, `IdJagSubjectContext` and `SamlNameIdSubjectIdentifier` renders no subject, tenant or NameID value.
  - **The comparison.** `IdJagSubjectIdentifierComparison.CompareAsync` compares under the rule's `Relation`, or `ComparisonRelation.Equal` when the rule names none, and answers in this order. A candidate of a format outside a non-empty `AcceptedFormats` is `Indeterminate`, basis `None`, and the association is not asked. A relation other than `Equal` is `Indeterminate`, basis `None`, and the association is not asked. Under `Optional`, an `iss_sub` candidate whose `iss` is the grant's validated issuer, in a grant that carries no `tenant`, `Holds`, basis `ProfileRule`, when its `sub` is ordinally the grant's `sub`, and with another `sub` it `Fails`, basis `ProfileRule`, when no association is wired. Every candidate the steps above leave, which under `Required` is every candidate compared under `Equal`, the one identical to the grant's subject included, takes the association's outcome, basis `ApplicationRule`, and is `Indeterminate`, basis `None`, when no association is wired. The association is called at most once, and an exception it throws reaches the caller. The decision follows from the outcome in one place: `Holds` is `Permit`, `Fails` is `Deny`, `Indeterminate` is `IndeterminateDeny` under `Required` and `Permit` under `Optional`, and an outcome outside the enumeration is `IndeterminateDeny`. A deny and an indeterminate-deny answer the wire with the same body, byte for byte; the record's `Outcome`, `Basis` and `Decision` tell them apart. The vocabulary of `Verifiable.Core.Relations` (`ComparisonRelation`, `ComparisonOutcome`, `ComparisonBasis`, `ComparisonDecision`, `ComparisonRecord`, `CompareDelegate`) is taken from the PIC Protocol drafts [PIC Prover and Verifier (pic-prover-verifier-02) §4.2](https://github.com/pic-protocol/pic-spec/blob/35b1d904aebf9438b0f25d7465d1d0b965d6656b/draft/0.2/rfc/pic-prover-verifier-spec.txt) and [PIC Sandboxed Execution (pic-sandboxed-execution-02) §2.4](https://github.com/pic-protocol/pic-spec/blob/35b1d904aebf9438b0f25d7465d1d0b965d6656b/draft/0.2/rfc/pic-lineage-guardrail-spec.txt), each of which states "It is not an RFC, an Internet Standard, or an official work item of any working group or standards body."; the library adopts their words and claims no conformance to the PIC Protocol.
  - **The application's turn.** For a candidate the association decides, the outcome it returns is the comparison's outcome whatever the candidate's members hold, so the obligations the texts place on the party that resolves a subject by the claim are the association's (`IdJagSubjectIdentifierRule.Association`). For a `saml-nameid` candidate, [draft-04 §3.2.2](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-ietf-oauth-identity-assertion-authz-grant-04#section-3.2.2): "The Resource Authorization Server MUST compare every member of the SAML NameID Subject Identifier that is part of the set of identifier fields it uses for subject resolution for that SAML issuer. The Resource Authorization Server MUST NOT resolve the subject using only the nameid value unless local policy explicitly defines nameid alone as the subject identifier for that SAML issuer.", and the same paragraph makes the `sp_name_qualifier` value part of the subject namespace of a NameID scoped to a Service Provider; [draft-04 §9.5](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-ietf-oauth-identity-assertion-authz-grant-04#section-9.5): "The Resource Authorization Server MUST use a SAML NameID sub_id only when the validated ID-JAG issuer is explicitly associated with the SAML issuer identified by sub_id.issuer through local configuration or trusted federation metadata." For an `email` candidate, [RFC 9493 §3.2.2.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9493#section-3.2.2.1): "Therefore, the recipient SHOULD apply its own canonicalization algorithm to incoming events in order to reproduce the translation done by the local email system." For an `aliases` candidate, [RFC 9493 §3.2.8](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9493#section-3.2.8): "Each Subject Identifier in the array MUST identify the same entity." For a `complex` candidate, [OpenID Shared Signals Framework 1.0 §3.3.1](https://openid.net/specs/openid-sharedsignals-framework-1_0.html#section-3.3.1): "All members within a Complex Subject MUST represent attributes of the same Subject Principal." An application that validates under `IdJagSubjectIdentifierRule.Default` and resolves a subject by the surfaced claim takes on [draft-04 §3.1](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-ietf-oauth-identity-assertion-authz-grant-04#section-3.1), [§3.2.2](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-ietf-oauth-identity-assertion-authz-grant-04#section-3.2.2) and [§9.5](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-ietf-oauth-identity-assertion-authz-grant-04#section-9.5) itself, and [RFC 9493 §4.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9493#section-4.1): "When processing a JWT with both JWT "sub" and "sub_id" Claims, implementations MUST NOT rely on both claims to determine the JWT Subject." At the mint the library writes the identifier the seam returns and chooses nothing in it, so the obligations of the party that chooses the identifier are the seam's (`TokenExchangeAuthorization.SubjectIdentifier`): [draft-04 §3.2.2](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-ietf-oauth-identity-assertion-authz-grant-04#section-3.2.2) has the IdP derive the members of a SAML NameID identifier from the NameID it would use for SSO to the target Resource Authorization Server, or from an equivalent configured mapping, and include each optional field exactly when the corresponding attribute is present; [draft-04 §9.6](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-ietf-oauth-identity-assertion-authz-grant-04#section-9.6): "The IdP Authorization Server SHOULD minimize the subject identifiers included in the ID-JAG to those required by the Resource Authorization Server."; [RFC 9493 §6.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9493#section-6.1): "When transmitting Subject Identifiers, the transmitter SHOULD take care that they are only transmitting multiple identifiers together when it is known that the recipient already knows that the identifiers are related" or "when correlation is essential to the use case."; and [RFC 9493 §3.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9493#section-3.2): "Since the Subject Identifier Format conveys semantic information, applications SHOULD choose the most specific possible format for the identifier in question." The grant's signature and the trust in its issuer are the application's too, established by its redeem seam before the validator runs, and so are the value rules named below.
  - **The association dictionary.** `IdJagSubjectAssociationDictionary` is the library's table-shaped association: a concurrent dictionary from each validated grant subject (`IdJagSubjectContext`, a sealed record compared by value, so an entry for one tenant is not an entry for another) to the `ImmutableArray<SubjectIdentifier>` the application knows that End-User by, wired as `Association = associations.CompareAsync` on an `IdJagSubjectIdentifierRule`. An entry is the local configuration of [draft-04 §9.5](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-ietf-oauth-identity-assertion-authz-grant-04#section-9.5) quoted above. Listing an identifier under a grant subject authorizes its namespace for the grant's issuer, against the refusal of [draft-04 §3.2.2](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-ietf-oauth-identity-assertion-authz-grant-04#section-3.2.2) when "the sub_id claim is not authorized by local policy for the validated ID-JAG issuer.", and states that it names the same End-User, [draft-04 §3.1](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-ietf-oauth-identity-assertion-authz-grant-04#section-3.1): "When both sub and sub_id are present, they MUST identify the same End-User." `IdJagSubjectAssociationDictionary.CompareAsync` answers in this order: a relation other than `Equal` is `Indeterminate`; a subject with no entry is `Indeterminate`, because an absent association decides nothing (this library's rule); an entry listing an identifier equal to the candidate `Holds`; any other candidate `Fails`. Two identifiers are equal when their formats are ordinally equal and their members structurally equal by `StructuralEquality.JsonEqual`, every member compared, which is the strictest reading of the sentence of [draft-04 §3.2.2](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-ietf-oauth-identity-assertion-authz-grant-04#section-3.2.2) quoted above; member names and string values compare ordinally whatever comparer a member map carries, the comparison [RFC 8259 §8.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8259#section-8.3) names interoperable: "Implementations that transform the textual representation into sequences of Unicode code units and then perform the comparison numerically, code unit by code unit, are interoperable in the sense that implementations will agree in all cases on equality or inequality of two strings." A listed member map that holds one name twice (possible only under a comparer that tells two strings of the same code units apart) equals no candidate. An `aliases` candidate is compared as a whole and not expanded, and an `email` value is compared without canonicalization (deviations below). An application that resolves a subject by fewer members, by a single alias or by a canonicalized `email` supplies its own delegate. The application adds, replaces and removes entries at any time, under traffic, through the inherited operations; each comparison reads the entry at its moment. A listed identifier and every map and list reachable from its members are not changed after listing, and an entry changes by replacing its array with new identifiers, so a comparison never observes a half-written entry; the dictionary holds no cache, no expiry and no bound. A subject with no entry is undecided, which permits under `Optional` and refuses under `Required` (the comparison above), so an application that resolves the subject by the surfaced `sub_id` pairs the dictionary with `Required`. Its debugger display and `ToString` show the number of entries only.
  - **Deviations.** Each is named with its reason.
    - The three refusals of [draft-04 §3.2.2](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-ietf-oauth-identity-assertion-authz-grant-04#section-3.2.2) are made for every format the rule accepts, where the clause names "a SAML NameID Subject Identifier" (`IdJagSubjectIdentifierPresence.Required`). The reason is that the same section lets the server resolve subjects by any format it supports ("The Resource Authorization Server MAY use sub_id for subject resolution when it supports the indicated Subject Identifier Format."), so the rule, not the format, says whether the server requires the claim: `AcceptedFormats` names the formats the server resolves subjects by, and the three cases read the same for each of them.
    - Under `Required` the association decides every claim, the one identical to the grant's subject included, and with no association wired the grant is refused as undecided (`SubjectIdentifierUndetermined`). The reason, stated in the remarks of `IdJagSubjectIdentifierComparison.CompareAsync`: whether the claim is authorized by local policy is the application's.
    - Under `Optional` an undecided comparison permits, and the record keeps `Indeterminate`, basis `None`, beside the decision `Permit`: a departure from [PIC Sandboxed Execution (pic-sandboxed-execution-02) §2.6](https://github.com/pic-protocol/pic-spec/blob/35b1d904aebf9438b0f25d7465d1d0b965d6656b/draft/0.2/rfc/pic-lineage-guardrail-spec.txt)'s "Failure, unavailable policy, indeterminate result, inconsistent semantic mapping, or missing committed inputs produce deny." The reason is that the claim is OPTIONAL in [draft-04 §3.1](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-ietf-oauth-identity-assertion-authz-grant-04#section-3.1); the library follows that sentence where the claim is required and not where it is optional (`IdJagSubjectIdentifierRule.Presence`).
    - A rule that names a relation other than `ComparisonRelation.Equal` is accepted as given, and its comparison is recorded `Indeterminate`, basis `None`, without asking the association, so it permits under `Optional` and refuses under `Required`, where [PIC Prover and Verifier (pic-prover-verifier-02) §4.2](https://github.com/pic-protocol/pic-spec/blob/35b1d904aebf9438b0f25d7465d1d0b965d6656b/draft/0.2/rfc/pic-prover-verifier-spec.txt) reads "a Verifier MUST reject a PCA whose profile is unknown or does not define a deterministic comparison." The reason is that the `sub_id` profile defines equality only (`IdJagSubjectIdentifierRule.Relation`), so another relation is a comparison nothing decides, and its permit under `Optional` is the departure above.
    - `IdJagSubjectIdentifierRule.Default` judges nothing: a well-formed claim of any format is surfaced, one that is not well formed is surfaced as `null`, and no comparison is recorded. [draft-04 §3.2.2](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-ietf-oauth-identity-assertion-authz-grant-04#section-3.2.2): "The Resource Authorization Server MAY use sub_id for subject resolution when it supports the indicated Subject Identifier Format." The claim is OPTIONAL and its use a MAY, so this is a permitted choice and not a departure; an application that resolves a subject by the surfaced claim carries the obligations of the application's turn above.
    - With no association wired, an `iss_sub` claim whose `iss` is the grant's own issuer, in a grant that carries no `tenant`, and whose `sub` names another subject is refused (`Fails`, basis `ProfileRule`, `SubjectIdentifierMismatch`). No clause states that refusal. It is this library's reading of [draft-04 §3.1](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-ietf-oauth-identity-assertion-authz-grant-04#section-3.1)'s "When both sub and sub_id are present, they MUST identify the same End-User." with "A public subject identifier MUST be unique when scoped with issuer (iss+sub) for a single-tenant issuer", under which two subjects of one issuer are two End-Users unless the application says they are one. An application whose issuer hands out pairwise identifiers wires the association, which then decides, as [RFC 9493 §4.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9493#section-4.2) allows: "Similarly, the JWT's "sub" Claim and the Subject Identifier's "sub" member MAY be different."
    - The mint answers an identifier that is not well formed, a granted `resource` entry the resource indicator grammar refuses (`TokenExchangeAuthorization.Resource`), a granted `scope` the scope grammar refuses (`TokenExchangeAuthorization.Scope`), and a tenant relationship value of white space only (`TokenExchangeAuthorization.Tenant`, `AudienceTenant`, `AudienceSubject`), `server_error` with a fixed description, and not with an error of the request. The reason (`TokenExchangeAuthorization.SubjectIdentifier`, `Resource`, `Scope`, `Tenant`, `AudienceTenant`, `AudienceSubject`): each value is the authorization seam's, so a defective one is the server's own defect, and a grant carrying a defective resource, scope or tenant relationship value is one the redeem always refuses; the check runs before the grant's `jti` is generated, so no grant identifier is spent on it.
    - A `sub` of white space only is refused as `MissingSubject` before any `sub_id` rule runs. [RFC 7523 §3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7523#section-3) item 2: "The JWT MUST contain a "sub" (subject) claim identifying the principal that is the subject of the JWT." Reading a value of white space only as identifying no principal is this library's reading (`IdJagValidationFailureReason.MissingSubject`), the one its token factories hold when they refuse such a subject.
    - A `jti` of white space only is refused as `MissingJti`. [Draft-04 §3.1](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-ietf-oauth-identity-assertion-authz-grant-04#section-3.1) reads "jti: REQUIRED - Unique ID of this JWT as defined in Section 4.1.7 of [RFC7519]." and no sentence of Section 3.1 or Section 4.4.1 refuses a string of white space; the refusal is this library's fail-closed reading. The reason: such a value identifies no JWT, as a `sub` of white space only identifies no principal, and the replay defense the redeem seam hands the `jti` to keys on it (`IdJagValidationFailureReason.MissingJti`).
    - A time claim whose number names no instant a `DateTimeOffset` holds (an `exp`, `nbf` or `iat` before 0001-01-01T00:00:00Z or past 9999-12-31T23:59:59.9999999Z, a number beyond a 64-bit integer included) is refused as `InconsistentTemporalClaims`. [RFC 7519 §2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7519#section-2) makes such a number a NumericDate, and the refusal is this library's fail-closed reading. For the `exp`, [RFC 7523 §3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7523#section-3) item 4 reads "Note that the authorization server may reject JWTs with an "exp" claim value that is unreasonably far in the future."; for the `nbf` no sentence of the section reads so, and item 6 lets the server reject an `iat` "that is unreasonably far in the past" only. The reason: an instant the library cannot hold cannot be compared with the clock (`Rfc7523AssertionValidation`, `IdJagValidationFailureReason.InconsistentTemporalClaims`).
    - A `tenant`, `aud_tenant` or `aud_sub` claim that is present and is not a JSON string with at least one character that is not white space refuses the grant. The draft's part is the JSON string: [draft-04 §3.1](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-ietf-oauth-identity-assertion-authz-grant-04#section-3.1) defines "tenant: OPTIONAL - JSON string that represents the tenant identifier for a multi-tenant issuer", "aud_tenant: OPTIONAL - A JSON string that represents a Resource Authorization Server tenant identifier" and "aud_sub: OPTIONAL - The Resource Authorization Server's identifier for the End-User as defined in [OpenID.Enterprise]." and names no JSON type for `aud_sub`. This library's part is the refusal, of the empty string and of a string of white space only as of any other value that is no such string: a fail-closed reading that no sentence of [draft-04 §3.1](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-ietf-oauth-identity-assertion-authz-grant-04#section-3.1), [§4.4.1](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-ietf-oauth-identity-assertion-authz-grant-04#section-4.4.1) or [§6.3](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-ietf-oauth-identity-assertion-authz-grant-04#section-6.3) states. The reason: the tenant decides which namespace the subject is unique in, and a grant read as carrying no tenant is one the library's own comparison rule may decide. Reading `aud_sub` as such a string, as the claims beside it are, is this library's reading too. The members that state it: `IdJagValidationFailureReason.MalformedTenant`, `IdJagValidationFailureReason.MalformedAudienceTenant` and `IdJagValidationFailureReason.MalformedAudienceSubject`.
    - The format check makes no rule on a member's value, a departure from [RFC 9493 §3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9493#section-3)'s "Every member within a Subject Identifier MUST match the rules specified for that member by this specification or by a Subject Identifier's Identifier Format." Not checked: the `acct` URI of [RFC 9493 §3.2.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9493#section-3.2.1), the `addr-spec` and the deliverable mailbox of [§3.2.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9493#section-3.2.2), the formats of `iss` and `sub` of [§3.2.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9493#section-3.2.3), E.164 of [§3.2.5](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9493#section-3.2.5), the DID URL of [§3.2.6](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9493#section-3.2.6), the URI of [§3.2.7](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9493#section-3.2.7), the values of `jwt_id` and `saml_assertion_id` of [OpenID Shared Signals Framework 1.0 §3.5.1](https://openid.net/specs/openid-sharedsignals-framework-1_0.html#section-3.5.1) and [§3.5.2](https://openid.net/specs/openid-sharedsignals-framework-1_0.html#section-3.5.2), and the RFC 4001 form of each address of [§3.5.3](https://openid.net/specs/openid-sharedsignals-framework-1_0.html#section-3.5.3). The reason, which the remarks of `SubjectIdentifier.IsValidForKnownFormat` state with each rule's clause: the check covers the members present, their JSON types, the members a format does not describe and the reserved `format` member, and the value rules are the caller's to enforce.
    - An `aliases` identifier that repeats an identifier is admitted, beside [RFC 9493 §3.2.8](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9493#section-3.2.8)'s "SHOULD NOT contain exact duplicates". The reason, in the summary of the private `IsValidAliases` of `SubjectIdentifier`: the clause binds the party that writes the identifier, and a reader loses nothing by a repeated alias.
    - An identifier whose nesting of `aliases` identifiers and `complex` subjects is deeper than 32 is neither valid nor well formed (`SubjectIdentifier.IsValidForKnownFormat`, `SubjectIdentifier.IsWellFormed`), the identifier checked standing at depth one and each `aliases` element and `complex` member one level deeper; the bound counts identifiers, not JSON levels. No clause states the bound; it is this library's resource rule. The reason, in the remarks of `SubjectIdentifier.IsWellFormed`: an `aliases` identifier may hold a `complex` subject ([RFC 9493 §3.2.8](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9493#section-3.2.8) forbids only an element in the Aliases Identifier Format) and a `complex` subject an `aliases` identifier ([OpenID Shared Signals Framework 1.0 §3.3](https://openid.net/specs/openid-sharedsignals-framework-1_0.html#section-3.3)), so the two formats nest each other without end; an identifier an application builds from its own objects, a cyclic object graph among them, is bounded by no parser, and a check that followed it without a bound would recurse until the stack is exhausted. The value is taken from the `MaxDepth` of 32 that the library's JSON readers of this family use for a document (`SsfJsonReadHelpers.DocumentOptions`), a depth that counts JSON levels; a parser's depth bounds the walk of an identifier read from the wire without keeping it inside this bound, and the Global Token Revocation parser reads a request at the default depth of 64, so a `sub_id` of 33 identifiers parses and the bound refuses it. One check walks a nested object once for each depth it needs, remembering by reference each nested object it found well formed and the deepest depth it found it at, so for an identifier that holds one nested object in several places the work of one check is linear in the size of its distinct nested objects, their members and elements, times the bound, not in its paths. That is the check's cost: a writer of the identifier writes every path, since JSON carries no references. The check reads an identifier's values as stable, finite data, what a JSON document holds: a value whose enumeration differs between two reads, or does not end, is outside what the check judges.
    - A REQUIRED member of the `saml-nameid` format is held non-empty, as are the REQUIRED members of `jwt_id` and `saml_assertion_id`. [draft-04 §3.2.1](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-ietf-oauth-identity-assertion-authz-grant-04#section-3.2.1) describes `issuer` and `nameid` each as "A JSON string containing" its value; the non-empty rule is this library's reading (`SubjectIdentifier.IsValidForKnownFormat`, `SamlNameIdSubjectIdentifier.TryFrom`), after the pattern of the formats of [RFC 9493 §3.2](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9493#section-3.2), whose required members are "REQUIRED and MUST NOT be null or empty".
    - `IdJagSubjectAssociationDictionary` compares an `aliases` candidate as one identifier, whole: it holds only against a listed `aliases` identifier equal to it, and an element that is listed alone does not make it hold. That is this library's reading against the intent [RFC 9493 §3.2.8](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9493#section-3.2.8) states for the format: "It is intended for use when a variety of identifiers have been shared with the party that will be interpreting the Subject Identifier, and it is unknown which of those identifiers they will recognize or support." The reason: an `aliases` candidate whose elements this server lists under two different grant subjects would, compared element by element, hold for either of them. An application that resolves a subject by any single alias supplies its own delegate (`IdJagSubjectIdentifierRule.Association`).
    - `IdJagSubjectAssociationDictionary` compares an `email` value ordinally, the domain part included, and applies no canonicalization, where [RFC 9493 §3.2.2.1](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9493#section-3.2.2.1) reads "Therefore, the recipient SHOULD apply its own canonicalization algorithm to incoming events in order to reproduce the translation done by the local email system." The reason: the canonicalization reproduces the local email system's translation, which the application knows and the library does not. An application that canonicalizes supplies its own delegate (`IdJagSubjectIdentifierRule.Association`), which canonicalizes the value before it compares.
  - Proven by `SubjectIdentifierFormatRulesTests`, the format rules: `AKnownFormatCarriesNoMemberItsFormatDoesNotDescribe`, `ARequiredMemberIsANonEmptyString`, `AnAliasesIdentifierHoldsOneOrMoreNonNestedIdentifiers`, `TheFormatMemberIsNotAMemberOfTheIdentifier`, `ASamlNameIdIdentifierCarriesItsRequiredAndOptionalMembers`, `AComplexSubjectHoldsSubjectIdentifiersOfAnyFormatTheCatalogAdmits`, `AnAliasesIdentifierMayRepeatAnIdentifier` (the duplicates admitted), `AnAliasesIdentifierAdmitsAComplexSubjectAsAnElement` (the letter of RFC 9493 Section 3.2.8), the pin `AnAliasesIdentifierRefusesANestedAliases`, `TheNestingOfAliasesAndComplexIsBounded` (a chain of 32 identifiers admitted, which proves the admission reaches the bound, and one of 33 refused, which proves the bound, the walk reading the innermost identifier in both), `ACyclicIdentifierGraphIsRefusedWithoutExhaustingTheStack` (the bound ends the walk of a the redeem: `RedeemSurfacesSamlNameIdSubjectIdentifier`, `EqualityHoldsForAnIssSubRestatingTheValidatedSubjectAsync`, `EqualityFailsForAnIssSubNamingAnotherSubjectOfTheValidatedIssuerAsync` and
